586	## Phase 1 — Earned patches (the core rework)
587	
588	Replace the blind seed patch with a patch **grown face-by-face under
589	validation**, promoting the surface type as the accumulated support allows.
590	Full design + math in DEVELOPMENT_PLAN ("The Robustness Rework", Phase 1
591	design). Commit semantics deliberately unchanged: earned (type, params,
592	patch) hands off to the existing \`grow_from\` flood/refit path, and every
593	commit gate (\`MIN_AXIAL_SWEEP_DEG\`, torus ratio, \`MIN_*_FACES\`,
594	\`resolve_claims\`) keeps its exact meaning.
595	
596	- **1.1 Closed-form initializers, unit-tested first** (synthetic strips/tori;
597	  the corpus's exactness precedents — r = 0.99998-style — make ~1e-9
598	  assertions appropriate):
599	  - Cylinder: axis = n₁ × n₂ from two admitted faces with non-parallel
600	    normals (exact on strip/quad tessellation), then \`fit_circle_for_axis\`
601	    (cylindrical.rs — exists, measured well-conditioned on 40–65° arcs).
602	    Guard with a numeric conditioning floor on |n₁×n₂|, documented as
603	    validity-not-threshold.
604	  - Sphere: normal lines anchored at facet **circumcenters** (exact through
605	    the centre for inscribed facets); centre = common-perpendicular midpoint
606	    of two lines; radius = mean distance.
607	  - Cone: existing normal-covariance axis over the earned patch
608	    (\`smallest_eigenvector_3x3\`) + \`axis_normals_consistent\`.
609	  - Torus (**time-boxed — skip without blocking the phase if conditioning
610	    fails**): axis from the meridian-coplanarity system
611	    \`(pᵢ − a)·(nᵢ × d) = 0\` over ≥4 faces (bilinear; solve by alternation or
612	    pairwise elimination; rank-deficient on sphere/cylinder data by
613	    construction — ladder order and a conditioning floor must precede it);
614	    then (R, r) via the existing \`fit_circle_2d\` profile fit (toroidal.rs).
615	- **1.2 The earn loop.** Replace \`seed_patch\` + \`condition_patch\` inside
616	  \`grow\` (compete.rs): BFS from the seed; admit a frontier face iff it
617	  validates the current fit at vtol (\`face_validates\`); on failure attempt
618	  **promotion** in ladder order (plane → cylinder/cone or sphere → torus),
619	  refitting accepted+candidate and requiring **all already-accepted faces**
620	  to validate under the promoted fit; if nothing validates, the face is
621	  across a seam — exclude it and **record it as a frontier face** (Phase 2
622	  consumes these). **Branch on ambiguity** (e.g. two same-height cylinder
623	  faces validating as a sphere): grow both; false branches should self-prune
624	  within ~2 faces. Hand each surviving (type, patch, params) to the unchanged
625	  \`grow_from\`. Add counters (behind verbosity/env): NO-GROWTH count, branches
626	  spawned / died-within-2, promotions attempted/accepted, frontier faces.
627	- **1.3 Drift-triggered cylinder→torus promotion** (time-boxed). A bend is
628	  locally a valid cylinder, so admission never fails; the signal is
629	  **sign-consistent precession** of the fitted axis across successive refits
630	  as support accumulates — not a magnitude test (if a magnitude constant
631	  seems needed, stop and ask). If it works, \`promote_bend\` + \`BEND_*\` become
632	  Phase-4 deletion candidates; if not, keep them and move on.
633	- **1.4 Validate.**
634	  - *Initializer accuracy per provenance*: measure cylinder-axis error vs
635	    references across Parasolid strips AND the Phase-0 OCCT Delaunay meshes.
636	    If median error exceeds ~1° on a provenance, degrade that provenance to
637	    the covariance axis over the earned patch (still clean, still not blind).
638	    **Stop and ask** if even that underperforms the blind patch anywhere.
639	  - *Branch lifetime*: if branches persist past ~2 faces, validation is less
640	    sharp than believed — stop and ask before the cost model changes.
641	  - *NO-GROWTH count* (baseline 323 on stepped_block fine) should collapse
642	    toward zero.
643	  - *Acceptance*: probe per-type breakdowns equal-or-better corpus-wide;
644	    stepped_block fine ≥ today's 75-surface state; **stepped_block medium and
645	    part_rounded_cube coarse not worse** (the identifiability stress cases —
646	    the one place the blind patch did irreplaceable work); suite green;
647	    harness ≥ baseline.
648	- **1.5 Delete after ablation** (rule 4): \`condition_patch\`, \`seed_patch\`'s
649	  jet filtering, \`SEED_PATCH_CAP\`, \`MAX_PATCH_DEPTH\`, \`PATCH_RADII\`,
650	  \`PATCH_TRIM_ROUNDS\`, \`MIN_CONDITION_FACES\`; likely \`MAX_TORUS_STARTS\`.
651	  Report the constant count.
652	
653	**Explicitly NOT fixed by this phase — do not chase here**: stepped fine's 4
654	surplus surfaces, medium's remaining gap, hill_with_hole's junk torus
655	(Phase 5c), runtime (Phase 2).
656

    71), so it is not Delaunay-specific. **And it is not triangle count either**:
    OCCT fine has 7.8× Parasolid medium's triangles and does worse (97 vs 84);
    two Delaunay-family meshes at ~6.5K triangles land at 88 and 109 while
    Parasolid at 4.6K lands at 84. **The surplus is almost entirely PLANES** —
    18 at best, 232 at worst — while the 18 tori survive every provenance above
    ~3.7K triangles. Narrow flat regions between fillets are the failure, which
    is exactly what the blind patch straddles and what Phase 1's earned patch
    targets. Stop attributing this to Delaunay.
    Two more findings: **\`cone_tangent_planes\` agrees with Parasolid** (4 planes
    + 2 cones + 1 sphere), so OCCT's B-spline reading is provenance-specific, not
    the model's nature — Phase 5c should read it that way; and **\`nosecone\` is a
    NEW failure**, 10 surfaces vs 3, from ~3000 mm² of its flat base being
    absorbed into the cone *plus* the remainder fragmenting into six planes with
    byte-identical parameters (connectivity, not fitting — the
    'absorbing bridge facets' family in LEARNINGS.md, now a hard failure).
    **DONE 2026-07-30 after six runs.** \`tests/fusion_reexport/\`, 13 models × 31
    exports, 1.8 MB, all **31/31 verified** by the 0.5c gate (mesh vertices on
    the original reference surfaces, 1.8e-7–7.3e-6 mm). Wired into the suite:
    15 pass, 16 ignored with numbers. Densities are **numeric and identical to
    what \`tests/remesh\` asks of OCCT** — 0.5/0.1/0.02 mm surface deviation at
    30/15/6° — so Fusion-vs-OCCT is a controlled comparison, not a comparison of
    two vendors' preset names.
    **THE HEADLINE, at matched requested tolerances**: Fusion produces roughly
    HALF the triangles OCCT does for the same requested deviation (2948 vs 6464
    at medium; 17598 vs 35596 at fine) — so nominal tolerance settings are not
    comparable across tessellators even when numerically identical. And at fine,
    Fusion's 82 surfaces beat OCCT's 97 with half the triangles, while Parasolid
    fine still leads at 75 with 3× more.
=== 0.5c ===
  - (c) **Pair-verification gate**: **DONE 2026-07-29** — \`tools/verify_pairs.sh\`,
    versioned rather than an ad-hoc loop, because the ad-hoc loop **reported
    15/15 on a corpus that was entirely unusable**. Its hole: stage 1's vertex
    compare returns distance **exactly 0** for a vertex strictly *inside* the
    reference solid, so a mesh at the wrong scale or position passes trivially —
    measured on 3 metres-scale exports, where a 25 mm sphere spanning 0.025 mm
    reported 0.0 while a correct mesh of the same reference reports 1.1e-6. The
    gate now classifies **VERIFIED** (passes with a nonzero distance),
    **CONTAINED** (passes with exactly zero — suspect scale or placement), and
    **QUARANTINE** (fails), and reports the f32 position bound, which reveals
    coordinate scale directly (5.7e-10 mm for the metres sphere against 7.2e-7 mm
    for the correct one). Verified against the known-bad corpus: 0/31, catching
    all 28 failures *and* the 3 that the old loop passed. Apply to (b)'s wild
    pairs, where the rate is the actual measurement. **Stop and ask** if most
    wild pairs fail it.
- **0.6 Quantum clamp + report** **DONE 2026-07-29** — stage 1 reports
  \`float32 precision: position bound B mm (quantum Q mm), vertex tolerance T\`
  and warns when T < B; the CLI driver then raises the tolerance to B
  (\`stage1::effective_vertex_tolerance_mm\`; applied in the driver, not inside
  stage 1, because \`Config\` is immutable through the pipeline and ~40 sites
  read \`vertex_tolerance_mm\` — threading a derived value belongs with Phase
  3.2's audit).
  The bound is **not** \`max|coord| × 1.19e-7\`, honoring the recorded DO-NOT:
  it is \`max over vertices of ‖(ulpₓ, ulp_y, ulp_z)‖ / 2\`, the largest
  projection of the per-coordinate rounding box onto any direction. Measured on
  the off-origin corpus: bound 4.32e-5 mm where the true max vertex-to-surface
  distance is 3.93e-5 (bound holds, 0.91 tight) and the naive quantum says
  6.10e-5 — so the naive figure is 1.4× too loose here. Both are reported;
  only the bound is used.
  **The clamp is inert on the entire corpus, including at 1 m** (4.32e-5 <
  5e-5 default). It first bites past ~1.02 m, where the f32 exponent steps and
  the bound roughly doubles; pinned by a synthetic 5 m mesh in
  \`float32_position_bound_scales_with_distance_from_origin\`. Sweep diff after
  landing: no model changed.
```
</details>

```
### Phase 1 design: earned patches (replaces the blind patch)

Replace grab-then-repair with **grow-under-validation**: BFS from the seed,
admitting a frontier face only if it validates against the current fit at
vertex tolerance; when a face fails, attempt **promotion** up the ladder
(plane → cylinder/cone or sphere → torus), refitting accepted+candidate and
requiring *every already-accepted face* to still validate; if no type
validates, the face is across a seam — exclude it and record it as a
**frontier face** (Phase 2 consumes these). The patch can never straddle a
junction by construction, which is the exact property the blind patch lacks.
Commit semantics do not change: the earned (type, params, patch) goes to the
unchanged \`grow_from\` flood/refit path, and every existing commit gate
(\`MIN_AXIAL_SWEEP_DEG\`, torus ratio, \`MIN_*_FACES\`, \`resolve_claims\`) keeps
its meaning.

What makes promotion workable at tiny support — where a free least-squares fit
is hopeless (sagitta arithmetic: over a 7.5° arc on r = 0.5 the sag is
~0.001 mm against a 2e-5 mm quantum, so a free radius is ~2% ≈ 200× vtol
wrong) — is that **facet normals are exact** and give closed-form,
DOF-reducing initializers:

- **Cylinder — axis = n₁ × n₂.** A planar facet spanning two rulings of a
  cylinder has its normal exactly perpendicular to the axis, at any coarseness,
  so two facets with non-parallel normals give the axis exactly on
  strip/quad-tessellated surfaces. Radius+origin then come from
  \`fit_circle_for_axis\` (cylindrical.rs), measured well-conditioned on short
  arcs (r = 0.99998 / 0.99999 / 0.50008 from 65°/60°/40° arcs). **Provenance
  caveat:** a general Delaunay triangle's normal is *not* exactly
  perpendicular to the axis; measure axis error per provenance (Phase 0's OCCT
  meshes), and degrade to the normal-covariance axis over the earned patch
  where needed — still clean, still not blind.
- **Sphere — concurrent normal lines.** For any planar facet inscribed in a
  sphere, the line through the facet's **circumcenter** along its normal
  passes exactly through the sphere centre (equidistance). Two facets → centre
  at the near-intersection (common-perpendicular midpoint), radius = distance.
- **Cone** — the existing normal-covariance axis (\`smallest_eigenvector_3x3\`)
  over the earned patch, guarded by \`axis_normals_consistent\`, both existing.
- **Torus — meridian coplanarity, the "open case" closed.** Every torus
  normal line lies in the meridian plane through its point, which contains
  the axis — so each facet contributes one coplanarity constraint
  \`(pᵢ − a) · (nᵢ × d) = 0\` on the axis (point a, direction d; 4 DOF; the
  system is bilinear — solve by alternation or pairwise elimination). Four
  facets pin the axis; then (R, r) is a 2-D circle fit in (ρ, z) profile
  coordinates — \`fit_circle_2d\` already exists (toroidal.rs). **Caveats:**
  rank-deficient by construction on sphere data (any axis through the centre
  satisfies it) and consistent-but-degenerate on cylinder data, so ladder
  order and a conditioning floor must precede it; grid-aligned quads give
  near-exact meridian normals, Delaunay triangles do not. **Time-boxed**: unit
  test to ~1e-9 on synthetic tori before integrating; if conditioning does not
  pan out, skip it — direct torus seeding stays as today and Phase 1 is not
  blocked.

Two behaviours need more than validation failure:

- **Ambiguity → branch, don't choose.** Two same-height faces of a cylinder
  validate as a sphere centred on the axis (the hole→sphere trap at patch
  scale). Grow both branches; the false one should die within a face or two
  (the next face at a different height is off the sphere by ≈ Δ²/2R ≫ vtol).
  Instrument branch lifetime; persistent branches mean validation is less
  sharp than believed — stop and ask.
- **Bent tube → drift-triggered promotion.** A bend is locally a valid
  cylinder (sag of the bend over a 2 mm patch at R = 14 is ~3.6e-5 mm), so
  admission never fails and promotion never triggers on residual. The signal
  is **systematic fit drift**: along a straight tube successive refits are
  stationary; along a bend the fitted axis precesses with a consistent sign as
  support accumulates. Test drift as sign-consistency of successive axis
  deltas, *not* magnitude (a magnitude bar would be a new tuned constant —
  stop and ask if one seems needed). Success retires \`promote_bend\` and the
  \`BEND_*\` constants; failure keeps them, and that is fine.

**Deleted when this lands (after ablation evidence):** \`condition_patch\`
entirely, \`seed_patch\`'s jet filtering, \`SEED_PATCH_CAP\`, \`MAX_PATCH_DEPTH\`,
\`PATCH_RADII\`, \`PATCH_TRIM_ROUNDS\`, \`MIN_CONDITION_FACES\`, likely
\`MAX_TORUS_STARTS\` (multiple flood starts exist because the fit patch was
unreliable).

### Phase 2 design: distance-guided exploration

The 26122-face seed sweep costs 103 s on stepped_block fine and its
useful-work fraction is unknown (profile first — do not build this phase's
machinery if the profile says the cost is in fits or floods rather than the
sweep). The principled orderings and bounds, all derived rather than tuned:

- **Distance field**: per-face geodesic distance to the region boundary
  (multi-source Dijkstra over the dual graph, **centroid-gap weights in
  millimetres, not hops** — hop counts lie on graded meshes, which is what
  \`PATCH_RADII\` existed to patch). Seed **farthest-first** (Gonzalez maximin),
  replacing \`neighbor_jet_mismatch\` — the jet-based proxy for exactly this
  quantity.
- **Feedback, not detection**: tangent junctions cannot be *detected* (the
  measured-dead pivot; a tangent junction is locally the *smoothest* place on
  the mesh — an anti-edge), but they are *discovered exactly* by Phase 1's
  excluded frontier faces. Add discovered frontiers as new distance sources
  incrementally. The only trustworthy edge map is the one the fits produce.
- **Duplicate abort**: a seed whose reachable ball lies entirely inside one
  committed surface can only refit that surface — skip it. Topological, a
  strict generalization of \`interior_of_claims\`, no numeric tolerance.
- **Acceptance discipline**: ordering must not change results. Bit-identical
  probe output, or every diff explained; order-dependence is itself the
  fragility being removed — stop and surface it.
```
</details>

**Next is Phase 1.1 — closed-form initializers, unit-tested first** ([TODO.md:596](TODO.md:596), design math in [DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md:1710](DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md:1710)). Four small, independent pieces, each asserted to ~1e-9 on synthetic strips/tori *before* anything touches the pipeline:

- **Cylinder**: axis = n₁ × n₂ from two admitted facets, then the existing `fit_circle_for_axis` ([cylindrical.rs:43](src/stage2/cylindrical.rs:43)). Needs a conditioning floor on |n₁×n₂|, documented validity-not-threshold.
- **Sphere**: normal lines anchored at facet circumcenters, centre = common-perpendicular midpoint.
- **Cone**: existing `smallest_eigenvector_3x3` ([mod.rs:430](src/stage2/mod.rs:430)) + `axis_normals_consistent` — mostly wiring.
- **Torus**: meridian-coplanarity axis. **Time-boxed** — rank-deficient on sphere data by construction; if conditioning doesn't pan out, skip it without blocking the phase.

Then 1.2 replaces `seed_patch`/`condition_patch` inside `grow` ([compete.rs:1107](src/stage2/compete.rs:1107), [compete.rs:1027](src/stage2/compete.rs:1027)) with the earn loop.

Three Phase-0 findings that should shape how I start:

1. The `stepped_block_toroidal` surplus is **not Delaunay-specific and not triangle count** — all three tessellators fail, and the surplus is almost entirely *planes*, i.e. the narrow flat regions between fillets that the blind patch straddles. That's Phase 1's actual target, so 1.4's acceptance check should watch the plane counts, not the totals.
2. **OrcaCube is a crash, not a timeout** ([repair.rs:741](src/stage2/repair.rs:741), face covered 0 times), reached after 2263 tangency snaps almost all reporting `NO LOCUS: surfaces do not share an axis`. TODO says Phase 1 retires that class first.
3. Four of the nine cliffs are blind-patch constants on 1.5's deletion list — so "those four vanish, no new ones appear" is the success criterion, and I should re-run `tools/fragility.sh` at the end of the phase, not just the sweep.

One thing worth deciding before I write code: the Delaunay caveat on the cylinder axis. On strip/quad meshes n₁ × n₂ is exact; on general triangles it isn't, and 1.4 says to measure per provenance and degrade to the covariance axis where it's bad. I'd rather measure that *first* — a throwaway probe over the Parasolid vs. `tests/remesh` OCCT meshes — since if the OCCT median axis error is already >1°, the initializer is a covariance fallback everywhere and 1.1 shrinks considerably.

Want me to start with that provenance probe, or go straight into the unit-tested initializers in order?

1	//! Geometric comparison against a reference STEP — and the trap that made three
2	//! of these gates read zero for every input, right and wrong alike.
3	//!
4	//! # Never measure a distance against a solid
5	//!
6	//! \`BRepExtrema_DistShapeShape\` computes the **minimum** distance between two
7	//! shapes, and it reports **zero whenever one lies inside the other**. Point
8	//! against a solid therefore answers "is it inside?", not "how far is it from
9	//! the surface?", and shape against shape answers "do they overlap?", not "do
10	//! they agree?". Both are useless as accuracy gates, and neither announces
11	//! itself: a wrong answer and a perfect one both print \`0.000000e0\`.
12	//!
13	//! Measured 2026-07-29, all three found at once:
14	//!
15	//! * stage 1's vertex check passed a mesh written 1000× too small — a 25 mm
16	//!   sphere spanning 0.025 mm sat wholly inside its own reference and reported
17	//!   a distance of exactly 0. Its centroid check was worse than useless: an
18	//!   inscribed tessellation puts every facet centroid *inside* the solid, which
19	//!   is precisely the sag the check exists to measure, so it read 0 on convex
20	//!   geometry everywhere. One of the two recorded sag truth values was 4.3×
21	//!   understated as a result.
22	//! * stage 3.4 sampled a point per face against the reference solid, same trap.
23	//! * stages 3.6 and 4.1 compared our solid to the reference solid and gated on
24	//!   the result. Two solids that overlap at all are at distance zero, so those
25	//!   gates never fired. Measured on a decomposition with 9 surfaces against a
26	//!   reference 26 and *no planes at all*: both printed \`0.000000e0\`, exactly as
27	//!   for a perfect model.
28	//!
29	//! # What to use instead
30	//!
31	//! * A point against a boundary: [\`face_compound\`], then the point-distance
32	//!   helpers in [\`crate::stage1\`]. A compound of faces has no inside.
33	//! * Two shapes against each other: [\`boundary_deviation\`]. Note that swapping
34	//!   in face compounds is **not** sufficient here — the minimum distance
35	//!   between two boundaries that touch anywhere is still zero. It samples the
36	//!   reference's surface and measures each point to ours, which is also the one
37	//!   direction nothing else checks.
38	//!
39	//! If you add a comparison, ask what your metric returns for a *grossly* wrong
40	//! input. If that is indistinguishable from a right one, the metric is wrong.

42	use opencascade_sys::{b_rep, b_rep_mesh, top_abs, top_exp, top_loc, topo_ds, OwnedPtr};
43	
44	/// A compound of every face of \`shape\` — the correct target for a
45	/// point-to-boundary distance.
46	///
47	/// A solid is not: see the module docs. Stage 3 builds edge and vertex
48	/// compounds for its own compares for the same reason.
49	pub fn face_compound(shape: &topo_ds::Shape) -> OwnedPtr<topo_ds::Compound> {
50	    let builder = topo_ds::Builder::new();
51	    let mut compound = topo_ds::Compound::new();
52	    builder.make_compound(&mut compound);
53	    let mut explorer = top_exp::Explorer::new_shape_shapeenum2(
54	        shape,
55	        top_abs::ShapeEnum::Face,
56	        top_abs::ShapeEnum::Shape,
57	    );
58	    while explorer.more() {
59	        builder.add(compound.as_shape_mut(), explorer.value());
60	        explorer.next();
61	    }
62	    compound
63	}
64	
65	/// Triangulation deflection used by [\`boundary_deviation\`], as a fraction of
66	/// the tolerance being tested against.
67	///
68	/// The deviation is sampled at triangulation nodes, so its resolution is the
69	/// deflection it meshes at. A tenth of the tolerance keeps the discretization
70	/// an order below the quantity being gated. This is a numerical-accuracy choice
71	/// for a diagnostic, not a tuned threshold: it trades runtime for resolution and
72	/// changes no decision except by making the measurement sharper. It also tracks
73	/// \`--surface-tolerance\`, so tightening that in Phase 3 sharpens this for free.
74	const DEVIATION_MESH_FRACTION: f64 = 0.1;
75	
76	/// Sample points: every triangulation node of every face of \`shape\`.
77	///
78	/// The shape must already be meshed. Node coordinates are in the face's local
79	/// frame, so the face location has to be applied — see \`viz.rs\`, which reads
80	/// triangulations the same way.
81	fn triangulation_nodes(shape: &topo_ds::Shape) -> Vec<[f64; 3]> {
82	    let mut points = Vec::new();
83	    let mut explorer = top_exp::Explorer::new_shape_shapeenum2(
84	        shape,
85	        top_abs::ShapeEnum::Face,
86	        top_abs::ShapeEnum::Shape,
87	    );
88	    while explorer.more() {
89	        let face = topo_ds::face_shape(explorer.value());
90	        let mut location = top_loc::Location::new();
91	        let tri_handle = b_rep::Tool::triangulation(face, &mut location, 0);
92	        if tri_handle.is_null() {
93	            explorer.next();
94	            continue;
95	        }
96	        let tri = tri_handle.get();
97	        let has_location = !location.is_identity();
98	        for i in 1..=tri.nb_nodes() {
99	            let node = tri.node(i);
100	            let (mut x, mut y, mut z) = (node.x(), node.y(), node.z());
101	            if has_location {
102	                location
103	                    .transformation()
104	                    .transforms_real3(&mut x, &mut y, &mut z);
105	            }
106	            points.push([x, y, z]);
107	        }
108	        explorer.next();
109	    }
110	    points
111	}
112	
113	/// How far the reference's boundary strays from ours: the maximum distance from
114	/// any point on the reference surface to our nearest surface.
115	///
116	/// This is the direction nothing else in the pipeline measures. Stage 1 and
117	/// stage 3.4 both go ours-to-reference, which cannot notice a reference feature
118	/// we failed to reproduce — our points can all sit perfectly on the reference
119	/// while whole regions of the reference have nothing of ours near them. Running
120	/// it the other way catches exactly that.
121	///
122	/// Built from sampled points against a face compound rather than from
123	/// \`DistShapeShape\` on the two shapes, for the reason in the module docs: a
124	/// minimum distance between overlapping shapes is zero, and between two
125	/// boundaries that touch anywhere it is also zero. \`BRepExtrema_ShapeProximity\`
126	/// was tried first and rejected — its "minimal thickness of a layer containing
127	/// both shapes" reported 13.2 mm for a hemisphere whose volume matched its
128	/// reference to 1.2e-7, so whatever it measures, it is not this.
129	///
130	/// Returns \`None\` if the reference cannot be sampled, which callers must treat
131	/// as "not measured" rather than as agreement.
132	pub fn boundary_deviation(
133	    ours: &topo_ds::Shape,
134	    reference: &topo_ds::Shape,
135	    tolerance: f64,
136	) -> Option<f64> {
137	    let deflection = (tolerance * DEVIATION_MESH_FRACTION).max(1.0e-6);
138	    let _mesh = b_rep_mesh::IncrementalMesh::new_shape_real_bool_real_bool(
139	        reference,
140	        deflection,
141	        false,
142	        0.5, // ~28.6 degrees, matching the corpus generator's tessellation angle
143	        false,
144	    );
145	    let samples = triangulation_nodes(reference);
146	    if samples.is_empty() {
147	        return None;
148	    }
149	    let ours_faces = face_compound(ours);
150	    let (max_dist, _) = crate::stage1::max_distance_to_shape(&samples, ours_faces.as_shape());
151	    Some(max_dist)
152	}

1	# Brepper - STL to STEP Converter
2	
3	Convert triangulated STL meshes from CAD exports into parametric STEP files with fitted analytic and freeform surfaces.
4	
5	## Usage
6	
7	\`\`\`
8	brepper - Convert STL mesh to STEP with fitted surfaces
9	
10	USAGE:
11	    brepper [OPTIONS] <input.stl> [-o <output.step>]
12	
13	REQUIRED:
14	    <input.stl>              Input STL file (binary or ASCII)
15	
16	GENERAL OPTIONS:
17	    -o <step>, --output=<step>  Output STEP file
18	    --stl-units=<units>      Units used by the STL file (mm, cm, m, in, ft, um; default: mm)
19	    --step-units=<units>     Units to use in exported STEP file (default: mm)
20	    --compare=<step>         STEP file to compare to at each step
21	    --vertex-tolerance=<value>  Fitting tolerance in STL units (default: 5e-5)
22	    --surface-tolerance=<value> Surface-to-face offset tolerance in STL units (default: 0.4)
23	    --angular-tolerance=<deg>   Max dihedral angle between adjacent triangles on the same surface (default: 25)
24	    -v, --verbose[=LEVEL]    Verbosity level: 1 = stage summaries, 2 = per-hypothesis details,
25	                             3 = commit details/stats/profiles, 4 = per-seed details, 5 = BFS face trace
26	    -q, --quiet              Suppress non-error output
27	    --strict-mesh            Fail on mesh defects instead of repairing them (dropping
28	                             duplicate-vertex and coincident faces, stitching T-junction
29	                             slivers, unifying winding, reorienting inverted shells)
30	    --debug                  Enable debug output and intermediate files
31	    --stage=<stage>          Stop after stage, e.g. 2.2 (default: 4.1). --stage=2 stops after all of stage2
32	                             Stage 2 sub-stages (partition-then-classify rewrite): 2.1 sharp-edge
33	                             classification + jet prior, 2.2 smooth-region extraction, 2.3 per-region
34	                             surface ladder, 2.4 resolution & repair, 2.5 selected surfaces
35	    --viz-stages=<stages>    Open interactive 3D visualization at specified stages (e.g. 2.1,2.2)
36	    --viz-stages-level=<N>   Viz detail level: 1 = committed hypotheses (default), 2 = region colors
37	\`\`\`
38	
39	### Examples
40	
41	\`\`\`bash
42	# Full conversion
43	./target/debug/brepper input.stl -o output.step
44	
45	# Just load and validate mesh (stage 1.2)
46	./target/debug/brepper input.stl --stage 1.2 -v
47	
48	# Compare mesh against reference STEP at each stage
49	./target/debug/brepper input.stl --compare reference.step --stage 1 -v
50	\`\`\`
51	
52	See [DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md](DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md) for full documentation.
53	
54	### Tolerance Settings
55	
56	#### Surface Tolerance
57	
58	This is how far the surface of a mesh triangle is allowed to deviate from the surface it is approximating.
59	
60	Onshape calls this "Chordal tolerance (mm)", and the preset values are:
61	- Coarse: 0.24 mm
62	- Medium: 0.12 mm
63	- Fine: 0.06 mm
64	
65	Fusion 360 calls this "Surface Deviation", and the preset values seem to vary based on model size:
66	- Low: 1x
67	- Medium: 2.5x
68	- High: 8x
69	
70	#### Angular Tolerance
71	
72	This is the maximum angle between adjacent triangle faces that approximate a smooth surface.
73	
74	Onshape calls this "Angular deviation (deg)", and the present values are:
75	- Coarse: 12.5 deg
76	- Medium: 6.25 deg
77	- Fine: 2.5 deg
78	
79	Fusion 360 calls this "Normal Deviation", and the preset values are:
80	- Low: 30.0
81	- Medium: 15.0
82	- High: 10.0
83	In theory "Normal Deviation" should be the maximum angular error of the face relative to the surface normal, but in practice it seems to result in the given maximum angular deviation.
84	
85	### OCCT-Generated Test Models
86	
87	Models in \`tests/occt/\` and \`tests/nurbs/\` are generated directly via OCCT by
88	the \`gen-test-models\` binary (\`./scripts/generate_occt_models.sh\`) — used for
89	models that ccad cannot express. \`tests/occt/\` holds analytic models such as
90	\`hollow_cube\` (a solid with a fully enclosed internal void, for multi-shell
91	solid support). \`tests/nurbs/\` holds freeform models (exact B-spline patches,
92	conic/B-spline extrusions) exercising the stage 2.7 NURBS fallback; all 8
93	pass full \`--compare\` — see \`tests/nurbs/README.md\`. The generated STL/STEP pairs are committed.
94	
95	### Generating Test Models
96	
97	Some tests use models generated via [CodeCAD](https://codecad.xyz/). The generated STL/STEP files are committed to the repo, so CodeCAD is not required to run tests.
98	
99	To add or modify these models:
100	1. Install CodeCAD
101	2. Edit files in \`tests/ccad/parts/\` — see \`tests/ccad/README.md\` for primitive conventions and placement rules
102	3. Run \`./tests/ccad/generate_models.sh\`
103	
104	**Important:** \`box(w,d,h)\` places a corner at the origin, not centered. Use \`center_xyz()\` to center. Each part file includes analytical surface area and volume in its header comments.
105	
106	### Interactive Debugging Visualization
107	
108	Use \`--viz-stages\` to open a 3D window and step through BFS hypothesis deduction interactively.
109	Use \`--viz-stages-level\` to control how often the visualization pauses:
110	- Level 1 (default): Pauses at each committed hypothesis
111	- Level 2: Also pauses at each accepted seed before BFS expansion
112	- Level 3: Also pauses at each face accepted during BFS expansion
113	
114	\`\`\`bash
115	# Visualize planar and cylindrical hypothesis stages (committed hypotheses only)
116	brepper input.stl --viz-stages=2.1,2.2
117	
118	# Visualize with seed-level detail
119	brepper input.stl --viz-stages=2.2 --viz-stages-level=2
120	
121	# Visualize all stage 2 hypothesis types with STEP comparison overlay
122	brepper input.stl --compare reference.step --viz-stages=2.1,2.2,2.3
123	\`\`\`
124	
125	At each BFS step, the window shows the STL mesh with highlighted faces: seed faces in green, previously accepted faces in blue, the just-accepted face in dark blue, and BFS queue frontier edges in red. Cylindrical and spherical hypotheses also display translucent geometry overlays. When \`--compare\` is provided, the STEP surface is shown at 35% opacity for reference.
126	
127	| Action | Control |
128	|--------|:--------|
129	| Next step | \`Space\` |
130	| Skip to next seed/hypothesis | \`Shift+Space\` |
131	| Skip to face seed | \`Click\` on a face |
132	| Quit visualization | \`Q\` |
133	
134	Supported stages: \`2.1\` (sharp edges + jet prior), \`2.2\` (smooth regions), \`3.3\` (edge curves), \`3.4\` (faces), \`3.5\` (shells), \`3.6\` (solids). (The old per-type BFS viz stages are retired with the stage-2 rewrite.)
135	
136	
137	## Viewer
138	
139	\`viewer\` is an interactive 3D viewer for STL and STEP files.
140	
141	\`\`\`
142	USAGE:
143	    viewer [OPTIONS] <file>...
144	
145	ARGUMENTS:
146	    <file>...   One or more STL or STEP files to view (detected by extension)
147	
148	OPTIONS:
149	    --deflection <value>          Linear deflection for STEP tessellation (default: 0.1)
150	    --angular-deflection <value>  Angular deflection for STEP tessellation in degrees (default: 28.6)
151	\`\`\`
152	
153	Multiple files can be loaded simultaneously; each is rendered in a distinct color.
154	
155	### Examples
156	
157	\`\`\`bash
158	# View a single STEP file
159	./target/release/viewer part.step
160	
161	# View a STEP and an STL side by side
162	./target/release/viewer reference.step output.stl
163	
164	# Finer tessellation for curved surfaces
165	./target/release/viewer part.step --deflection 0.01 --angular-deflection 5.7
166	\`\`\`
167	
168	### Controls
169	
170	| Action | Control |
171	|--------|:--------|
172	| Rotate (around model center) | Left-click + drag |
173	| Pan | Right-click + drag |
174	| Zoom (at cursor) | Scroll wheel |
175	| Quit | \`q\` or \`Esc\` |
176	| Toggle perspective/orthographic | \`p\` |
177	| Toggle wireframe edges | \`e\` |
178	| Toggle soft (mesh) edges | \`Shift+E\` |
179	| Toggle solid faces | \`s\` |
180	| Toggle hidden-edge removal | \`h\` |
181	
182	Rendering uses PBR shading with ambient and two directional lights. Sharp feature edges (boundary edges and edges where the dihedral angle exceeds ~45°) are drawn as a solid wireframe overlay. Softer mesh edges (dihedral angle below ~45°) are drawn at 50% opacity and can be toggled independently with \`Shift+E\`. Zooming targets the point under the mouse cursor.
183	
184	### Running Tests
185	
186	\`\`\`bash
187	# Run all tests (unit + integration)
188	cargo test
189	
190	# Run only integration tests
191	cargo test --test stage1_integration
192	\`\`\`
193	
194	Integration tests in \`tests/stage1_integration.rs\` verify that:
195	- All STL files in \`tests/manual/\`, \`tests/onshape/\`, \`tests/ccad/generated/\`, and \`tests/fusion/\` pass stage 1 mesh validation and coplanar triangle fusion
196	- All STL/STEP pairs pass \`--compare\` surface distance checks (after fusion)
197	- Bad files in \`tests/bad/\` fail with expected error types (degenerate faces, non-manifold edges, inconsistent winding, compare failures)
198	
199	Integration tests in \`tests/stage2_integration.rs\` verify that:
200	- Planar hypothesis deduction produces correct counts for known planar models (cube=6, wedge=6, chamfered cube=26)
201	- Cylindrical hypothesis deduction produces correct counts and properties (simple_cylinder=1 convex, block_with_hole=1 concave, pipe=2, two_holes=2 concave)
202	- Spherical hypothesis deduction produces correct counts and properties (simple_sphere=1 convex r=10, hemisphere=1 convex r=10, spherical_pocket=1 concave r=8, ball_on_cylinder=1 sphere+1 cylinder)
203	- Surface selection (stage 2.6) produces correct surface type counts for known models (block_with_hole=6 planar+1 cylindrical, pipe=2 planar+2 cylindrical, ball_on_cylinder=1 planar+1 cylindrical+1 spherical, hemisphere=1 planar+1 spherical, simple_sphere=1 spherical, cone_15x20=1 planar+1 conical, simple_cone=2 planar+1 conical)
204	- All STL/STEP pairs pass \`--compare\` validation at stages 2.1, 2.2, 2.3, and 2.6
205	- Deduced cylinder parameters (axis direction, axis position, radius) match STEP cylinder parameters for all cylindrical test models
206	- Deduced sphere parameters (center, radius) match STEP sphere parameters for all spherical test models
207	- Angular tolerance enforcement prevents spurious cylindrical/spherical hypotheses on planar-only models (e.g., cube faces meet at 90°, exceeding the 25° default)
208	Integration tests in \`tests/stage3_integration.rs\` verify that:
209	- Adjacency graph topology (faces, edges, vertices) is correct for known models (cube=6F/12E/8V, cylinder=3F/2E/0V, sphere=1F/0E/0V, etc.)
210	- Euler's formula V-E+F=2 holds for genus-0 models
211	- Edge validity, adjacency symmetry, and edge-face consistency are maintained
212	- All STL/STEP pairs pass \`--compare\` validation at stage 3.1, verifying that reconstructed edge boundary vertices lie on STEP edges and BRep vertices coincide with STEP vertices
213	- Tangency detection (stage 3.2) correctly identifies no tangent edges for models composed of planar, cylindrical, and spherical surfaces meeting at angles > 2°
214	- Edge curve computation (stage 3.3) produces 3D curves for all edges via surface-surface intersection, verified across all test models (cube, cylinder, hemisphere, ball_on_cylinder, block_with_hole, chamfered_cube, spherical_pocket)
215	- Face creation (stage 3.4) successfully creates OCCT \`TopoDS_Face\` objects for all test models via shared-edge topological assembly: every edge is one \`TopoDS_Edge\` shared by both adjacent faces, with per-face pcurves, seam edges, and degenerate pole edges built uniformly for all surface types. All STL/STEP pairs pass \`--compare\` validation at stage 3.4
216	- Shell construction (stage 3.5) assembles faces into shells directly from the shared-edge topology (closed by construction; no sewing) and produces correctly oriented shells. All STL/STEP pairs pass \`--compare\` validation
217	- Solid construction (stage 3.6) creates solids from shells via \`ShapeFix_Solid::SolidFromShell\`. Volume comparison against STEP reference and \`BRepExtrema_DistShapeShape\` distance checks pass for all test models
218	Integration tests in \`tests/stage4_integration.rs\` verify that:
219	- STEP output files are written successfully for cube, cylinder, and sphere models
220	- All STL/STEP pairs pass \`--compare\` validation at stage 4.1, verifying volume agreement and \`BRepExtrema_DistShapeShape\` distance between written output and reference STEP
221	- Missing output path produces the expected error
222	
223	
224	Bad test STL files can be regenerated with \`python3 scripts/generate_bad_tests.py\`.
225

1878	/// Extract cylinder parameters from a STEP file: returns Vec<(axis_dir, axis_origin, radius)>.
1879	fn extract_step_cylinders(step_path: &str) -> Vec<([f64; 3], [f64; 3], f64)> {
1880	    let shape = brepper::read_step_file(step_path).unwrap();
1881	
1882	    let mut cylinders = Vec::new();
1883	    let mut explorer = top_exp::Explorer::new_shape_shapeenum2(
1884	        &shape, top_abs::ShapeEnum::Face, top_abs::ShapeEnum::Shape,
1885	    );
1886	    while explorer.more() {
1887	        let face = topo_ds::face_shape(explorer.current());
1888	        let adaptor = b_rep_adaptor::Surface::new_face(face);
1889	        if adaptor.get_type() == geom_abs::SurfaceType::Cylinder {
1890	            let cyl = adaptor.cylinder();
1891	            let radius = cyl.radius();
1892	            let axis = cyl.axis();
1893	            let dir = axis.direction();
1894	            let loc = axis.location();
1895	            cylinders.push((
1896	                [dir.x(), dir.y(), dir.z()],
1897	                [loc.x(), loc.y(), loc.z()],
1898	                radius,
1899	            ));
1900	        }
1901	        explorer.next();
1902	    }
1903	
1904	    // Deduplicate: STEP files can have multiple faces on the same cylinder.
1905	    // Group by matching axis direction (parallel), axis position (colinear),
1906	    // and radius.
1907	    let mut unique: Vec<([f64; 3], [f64; 3], f64)> = Vec::new();
1908	    'outer: for (dir, origin, r) in &cylinders {
1909	        for (udir, uorigin, ur) in &unique {
1910	            // Axis directions must be parallel
1911	            let d = udir[0] * dir[0] + udir[1] * dir[1] + udir[2] * dir[2];
1912	            if d.abs() < 0.999 {
1913	                continue;
1914	            }
1915	            // Radii must match
1916	            if (ur - r).abs() > 0.01 {
1917	                continue;
1918	            }
1919	            // Axis origins must be colinear (distance between axes < tolerance)
1920	            let diff = [origin[0] - uorigin[0], origin[1] - uorigin[1], origin[2] - uorigin[2]];
1921	            let t = diff[0] * udir[0] + diff[1] * udir[1] + diff[2] * udir[2];
1922	            let perp_sq = diff[0]*diff[0] + diff[1]*diff[1] + diff[2]*diff[2] - t*t;
1923	            if perp_sq < 0.01 * 0.01 {
1924	                continue 'outer; // Already have this cylinder
1925	            }
1926	        }
1927	        unique.push((*dir, *origin, *r));
1928	    }
1929	    unique
1930	}

1932	/// Verify that each STEP cylinder has a matching hypothesis with compatible
1933	/// axis direction, axis position, and radius.
1934	fn assert_cylinders_match_step(
1935	    output: &stage2::Stage2Output,
1936	    step_path: &str,
1937	) {
1938	    let step_cylinders = extract_step_cylinders(step_path);
1939	    let hypotheses = cylinder_hypotheses(output);
1940	
1941	    assert_eq!(
1942	        hypotheses.len(), step_cylinders.len(),
1943	        "Expected {} cylinder hypotheses (from STEP), got {}",
1944	        step_cylinders.len(), hypotheses.len(),
1945	    );
1946	
1947	    for (i, (step_dir, step_origin, step_radius)) in step_cylinders.iter().enumerate() {
1948	        // Find a matching hypothesis
1949	        let matched = hypotheses.iter().any(|h| {
1950	            // Radius match
1951	            if (h.radius_at_apex - step_radius).abs() > 0.1 {
1952	                return false;
1953	            }
1954	            // Axis direction: must be parallel (or anti-parallel)
1955	            let d = h.axis_direction[0] * step_dir[0]
1956	                  + h.axis_direction[1] * step_dir[1]
1957	                  + h.axis_direction[2] * step_dir[2];

1959	                return false;
1960	            }

1964	                h.apex[1] - step_origin[1],
1965	                h.apex[2] - step_origin[2],
1966	            ];
1967	            let t = diff[0] * h.axis_direction[0]
1968	                  + diff[1] * h.axis_direction[1]
1969	                  + diff[2] * h.axis_direction[2];
1970	            let perp_sq = diff[0]*diff[0] + diff[1]*diff[1] + diff[2]*diff[2] - t*t;
1971	            perp_sq < 0.1 * 0.1
1972	        });
1973	
1974	        assert!(
1975	            matched,
1976	            "STEP cylinder {} (dir=[{:.3},{:.3},{:.3}], r={:.3}) has no matching hypothesis",
1977	            i, step_dir[0], step_dir[1], step_dir[2], step_radius,
1978	        );
1979	    }
1980	}

73	### Mesh provenance and the seam assumption
74	
75	Design Principle #2 — that a B-rep face boundary is always a chain of mesh
76	edges, tangent or not — is load-bearing for the entire pipeline, so this
77	records why it holds and where it stops holding. (Researched 2026-07-28.)
78	
79	**Why it holds: every B-rep faceter is edge-first.** They all discretize each
80	edge *once*, shared by both adjacent faces, and then fill each face's
81	parametric domain out to those fixed boundary polylines. This is not a
82	stylistic choice a vendor might change — it is how watertightness is
83	guaranteed. OCCT states it outright: "At the first step all edges from a face
84	are discretized… At the second step, the faces are tessellated", with "edges
85	tessellation data … used to form contours of target faces and passed as input
86	to the triangulation algorithm" and "each face is processed separately". ACIS
87	is documented as "maintaining the edge consistency between adjacent faces";
88	Parasolid's vertex-matching machinery exists so that neighbouring face meshes
89	meet exactly. A tangent edge is an edge in the *topology*, so it is discretized
90	like any other and no triangle can straddle it.
91	
92	**What the likely sources are.** For models destined for STL and print-sharing
93	sites:
94	
95	| Tool | Kernel | Faceter |
96	|------|--------|---------|
97	| Fusion 360, Inventor | ShapeManager (Autodesk's ACIS fork) | quad-tree gridding |
98	| SolidWorks, Onshape, Solid Edge, Shapr3D, Plasticity | Parasolid | per-face, vendor |
99	| FreeCAD | Open CASCADE | **Delaunay** (Watson) in UV space |
100	| Rhino | own | regular quad grid + recursive subdivision |
101	| Tinkercad, SketchUp, Blender, OpenSCAD | none — mesh/CSG native | n/a |
102	
103	Note the corpus is Onshape-heavy (Parasolid) plus CodeCAD, so it is *not*
104	representative of the faceter mix in the wild.
105	
106	**Delaunay does occur, and the assumption survives it.** FreeCAD — the
107	fastest-growing free option, and the same mesher brepper itself uses — meshes
108	with OCCT's \`BRepMesh\`, which is genuinely Delaunay. But it is a *constrained*
109	Delaunay whose constraints are the pre-discretized face boundaries, so seams
110	are preserved exactly as elsewhere. What differs between faceters is the
111	*interior* pattern: grid-based faceters (ACIS, Rhino, Parasolid) leave a
112	strongly structured, anisotropic mesh, Delaunay much less so. The practical
113	consequence is that **boundary preservation is dependable across all of them,
114	while sampling density and anisotropy are corroborating evidence only** — worth
115	remembering if the tessellation layout is ever used as a signal (it is not
116	today; stage 2 uses only dihedral angle and the curvature jet).
117	
118	**OpenSCAD and other mesh-native sources: a known gap, not handled.** These
119	have no B-rep at any stage — \`cylinder()\` is a prism and \`sphere()\` a
120	polyhedron from the moment they are created, with CSG run on polyhedra (CGAL,
121	or the newer Manifold backend). Consequences:
122	
123	- *The seam assumption holds trivially and uselessly.* Every facet is planar,
124	  so no triangle can span an orientation change — a stronger guarantee than any
125	  B-rep faceter gives. But **every** edge is a facet boundary, so nothing
126	  distinguishes an intended face boundary from a discretization step. The seam
127	  carries no information; only the curvature jump does (a constant dihedral
128	  through a fillet, dropping to 0° on the flat it meets).
129	- *There are no tangencies.* What was meant as a C¹ junction becomes a finite
130	  dihedral of one discretization step. OpenSCAD's fragment rule is
131	  \`n = ceil(max(min(360/$fa, r*2*PI/$fs), 5))\` with defaults \`$fa = 12\`,
132	  \`$fs = 2\`, so \`n\` caps at 30 and *every* curve carries a dihedral of at least
133	  12°.
134	- *Our \`--angular-tolerance\` of 25° is therefore the arbiter*, with a sharp
135	  crossover: \`$fn ≥ 15\` — or, on defaults, radius ≳ 4.5 mm, where
136	  \`ceil(πr) ≥ 15\` — reads as smooth and gets an analytic fit, while \`$fn ≤ 14\`
137	  reads as sharp and stays a prism. A \`$fn = 6\` hex nut correctly stays
138	  hexagonal; the near-ubiquitous \`$fn = 100\` reads as smooth.
139	- *Where a fit is attempted it will succeed easily.* OpenSCAD **inscribes** its
140	  polygons — the reason holes print undersized — so a prism's vertices lie
141	  exactly on the ideal cylinder of radius r, and vertex validation passes at
142	  ~zero residual. The binding check is the centroid one against
143	  \`--surface-tolerance\`: the sagitta is \`r(1 − cos(180/n))\`, which is 0.11 mm
144	  for r = 20, n = 30 (passes 0.4) but 0.55 mm for r = 100, n = 30 (fails, so
145	  the surface degrades to per-facet planes). Large-radius curves at default
146	  resolution are thus the expected failure mode.
147	- *The ambiguity is genuine, not a defect.* A 15-gon prism and a coarsely
148	  tessellated cylinder are the same mesh; nothing in it says which the designer
149	  meant. \`--angular-tolerance\` decides, which makes it a policy dial here
150	  rather than a measurement.
151	
152	We are not addressing this yet. When we do, the likely shape is a flag marking
153	the input as faceted-by-construction, or auto-detection — see TODO.md. Two
154	signals look promising for detection: an OpenSCAD revolution has a *constant*
155	dihedral of exactly 360/n around the whole curve (a B-rep tessellation's steps
156	vary and its density changes at seams), and its vertices sit exactly on the
157	circumscribed circle rather than straddling the true surface.
158	
159	## Dependencies
160	
161	| Library | Purpose | Version |
162	|---------|---------|---------|
```

1811	### Phase 0 design: the corpus and the instrument
1812	
1813	Three provenance sources, cheapest first, all scored against reference STEPs:
1814	
1815	1. **OCCT re-mesh of existing references** (automated, free ground truth).
1816	   \`gen_test_models\` already drives \`BRepMesh\` with deflection/angle settings
1817	   (\`write_tessellated_stl\`); add a mode that loads any reference STEP and
1818	   emits STL variants at several settings, plus **off-origin variants**
1819	   (translate the shape ~1 m before both STL and STEP output) so the float32
1820	   quantum question becomes testable. This adds constrained-Delaunay
1821	   provenance to a strip-heavy corpus.
1822	2. **Fusion 360 re-export** (user batch job): import reference STEPs, export
1823	   STL at low/medium/high. Different tessellator, same ground truth.
1824	3. **Wild STEP+STL pairs** (most representative, most care): harvest models
1825	   published with both a STEP/F3D and an STL (Thingiverse API / Printables /
1826	   GrabCAD, scripted or via a browser session). **Pair-verification gate**:
1827	   run stage 1 \`--compare\`; a pair counts as ground truth only if mesh
1828	   vertices lie on the STEP surfaces to within a few quanta — otherwise
1829	   quarantine as crash-test-only (the STL may be a different revision,
1830	   decimated, or repaired). Record the verification *rate*: it is itself the
1831	   first field measurement of the provenance premise ("vertices lie on the
1832	   true surface") in the wild. Keep licensing clean: store source URL,
1833	   author, license, and hash in a manifest; do not commit files whose license
1834	   does not permit redistribution (gitignore the external corpus, commit the
1835	   manifest).
1836	
1837	The instrument: a one-command sweep harness (per-model rows: per-type surface
1838	breakdown, \`--compare\` distances, volume error, runtime, exit status;
1839	machine-readable; saved baselines + diff mode) and a **fragility meter** that
1840	perturbs/ablates each stage-2 constant and reports which models change.
1841	
1842	### Phase 0 as built (2026-07-29)
1843	
1844	Steps 0.1, 0.2, 0.3 and 0.6 landed; step-by-step results with numbers are
1845	annotated in TODO.md. What belongs here is the reasoning and the findings that
1846	outlive the steps.
1847	
1848	**The corpus was measuring one tessellator and one distance from the origin.**
1849	\`tests/remesh/\` re-tessellates existing reference STEPs with OCCT's BRepMesh
1850	at three densities plus a ~1 m translation, so ground truth is unchanged and
1851	only provenance varies. That immediately produced the phase's headline result:
1852	\`stepped_block_toroidal\` fails at **every** OCCT density — 183 surfaces coarse,
1853	88 medium, 97 fine at 35596 triangles — while the Parasolid *fine* mesh of the
1854	same body passes at exactly 71. Fine yields *more* surfaces than medium, so it
1855	is not a resolution problem, and all 18 tori are recovered every time: what
1856	fragments is the planes and cylinders around them. The strip assumption is no
1857	longer a suspicion about the future; it is a measurement, and Phase 1's
1858	initializer caveat ("a general Delaunay triangle's normal is not exactly
1859	perpendicular to the axis") now has a model that demonstrates the cost.
1860	
1861	**The seeded random population found what fourteen hand-watched models could
1862	not.** \`tests/random/\` draws parameters where no constant was ever tuned, and
1863	three of its five families fail on the first sweep:
1864	
1865	- Filleted boxes collapse to **0 planes / 0 cylinders / 8 spheres / 1
1866	  B-spline** (reference: 6 + 12 + 8). Their fillet-to-face ratios are
1867	  0.03–0.10; \`rounded_cube_10_r2\`, the corpus's only filleted box and a model
1868	  that passes exactly, is 0.20. The corpus had been exercising a ratio 2–7×
1869	  larger than a typical part's, and at typical ratios one freeform patch
1870	  swallows the entire flat structure. This is the single most useful new target
1871	  for Phase 1: it is the blind patch's failure mode at scale.
1872	- A torus with r/R = 0.84 becomes **40 surfaces (17 cones + 20 spheres + 2 tori
1873	  + 1 B-spline)** for a one-face reference, while 0.36 and 0.10 are exact.
1874	  \`MAX_TORUS_RADIUS_RATIO = 0.7\` is rejecting a legitimate solid, not just
1875	  spurious fits. Phase 4.2 lists that guard under "expect these to still fire;
1876	  firing means keep, not tune" — this row is the counter-case to weigh.
1877	- A ball only slightly wider than its post (13.98 on 12.30) splits the sphere
1878	  into three and adds a plane.
1879	
1880	**The compare gates cannot see over-segmentation, so per-type counts are not
1881	optional.** \`rand_filleted_box_0\` passes stage 4.1 at 6.7e-3 relative volume
1882	with 9 surfaces against 26 and *no planes at all*; \`rand_cone_chain_1\` passes
1883	at 2.0e-3 with a cone replaced by a B-spline. Both report a stage 3.1 edge
1884	distance of exactly 0: that compare is one-sided — each of our edges to its
1885	nearest reference edge — so a decomposition with *fewer* edges satisfies it
1886	trivially, and the 1% volume gate is far too loose to notice. The measurement
1887	protocol's insistence on per-type breakdowns was already recorded (a 3-torus
1888	regression once hid behind an unchanged total of 71); this is a second,
1889	sharper reason. A two-sided edge compare would be a real improvement to the
1890	instrument, but it changes what "pass" means on every model and so belongs in
1891	its own step, not smuggled into Phase 0.
1892	
1893	**Pairing must be recorded, not inferred.** A re-mesh's reference lives in
1894	another directory, and searching \`tests/\` for a matching stem is both ambiguous
1895	(\`plate_with_hole_100x50.step\` exists under \`fusion/\` and \`onshape/\`) and
1896	filesystem-order dependent — the first version of the harness silently scored
1897	the OCCT re-mesh of the Onshape plate against the Fusion reference. The
1898	generator now writes \`tests/remesh/sources.tsv\` and the harness reads it.
1899	Any future generated corpus should do the same.
1900	
1901	**"The strip assumption" was the wrong diagnosis; narrow flat regions are the
1902	right one.** Phase 0.2 read the OCCT re-mesh result — \`stepped_block_toroidal\`
1903	failing at every Delaunay density while the Parasolid mesh passed — as the price

1914	
1915	Two smaller results from the same experiment. \`cone_tangent_planes\` decomposes

1919	And \`nosecone\`, which passes on the Onshape mesh at 3 surfaces, fails on the

1928	two shapes and reports **zero whenever one lies inside the other**. Point against

1940	  measure. **One of the two sag truth values this plan quotes was 4.3× low as a
1941	  result**: stepped_block fine measures 3.81e-3 mm, not 8.9e-4. rounded_cube
1942	  fine is unchanged at 9.5e-4, so the old numbers were not uniformly wrong,
1943	  which is exactly what made this hard to notice. Phase 0.7 calibrates its sag
1944	  estimator against these — use the new ones.
1945	- **Stage 3.4** sampled one point per face against the reference solid.
1946	- **Stages 3.6 and 4.1** gated on the distance between our solid and the
1947	  reference solid. Two versions of the same part always overlap, so those gates
1948	  never fired at all. Measured on a decomposition with 9 surfaces against a
1949	  reference 26 and *no planes whatsoever*: both printed the same 0 as a perfect
1950	  model, and only the volume check beside them did any work.
1951	
1952	The fix is not simply "use face compounds". For a point that works — a compound
1953	of faces has no inside. But the minimum distance between two *boundaries* that
1954	touch anywhere is still zero, so solid-vs-solid agreement needs a two-sided
1955	quantity: \`boundary_deviation\` uses \`BRepExtrema_ShapeProximity\` with an infinite
1956	tolerance, whose proximity value is the thinnest layer containing both shapes.
1957	That now separates the cases it could not before — 1.97e-2 mm for a correct
1958	model against 1.28e-1 mm for the 9-surface one. Its resolution floor is the
1959	triangulation deflection it meshes at, a tenth of the tolerance being gated, so
1960	it sharpens automatically if Phase 3 tightens \`--surface-tolerance\`.
1961	
1962	**The general rule, worth applying to any new check: ask what your metric
1963	returns for a grossly wrong input. If that is indistinguishable from what it
1964	returns for a right one, the metric is wrong.** Both failures here — these gates
1965	and the generator self-check that watched triangle counts while every file it
1966	wrote was unusable — are the same error: verifying a proxy, or verifying nothing,
1967	and reading the silence as success.
1968	
1969	**A corpus gate has to be run before the corpus is trusted, not after.** The
1970	first pair-verification pass reported 15 of 15 verified on a Fusion corpus that
1971	was written in metres — every file 1000× too small and useless — because it was
1972	an ad-hoc loop wrapping the stage-1 check described above. \`tools/verify_pairs.sh\`
1973	is that gate, versioned, and it is now a thin wrapper: stage 1 reports the true
1974	12.4875 mm and fails, so the gate needs no cleverness of its own. It briefly
1975	carried a heuristic for exactly-zero distances, which is deleted — a workaround
1976	for a bug is not worth keeping once the bug is fixed. It does still report the
1977	f32 position bound beside each result, because that reveals coordinate scale for
1978	free: 5.7e-10 mm for the metres sphere against 7.2e-7 mm for a correct mesh of
1979	the same model. This matters well beyond one bad export: 0.5b's wild pairs are
1980	*expected* to include decimated, repaired and rescaled meshes.
1981	
1982	The generator side needs the same discipline. Fusion's \`STLExportOptions\`
1983	inherits ambient state — \`unitType\` from the Design's default units, which for an
1984	imported STEP was metres, and \`printUtility\` from the last UI setting — so an
1985	export that does not set *every* property explicitly is not reproducible. That
1986	single fact explains two separate anomalies that cost four runs to untangle:
1987	byte-identical meshes across three refinement presets, and a silent 1000× scale
1988	error. The lesson generalizes past Fusion: when a generator's defaults come from
1989	the environment, pin them all, and verify the artifact rather than a proxy for it.
1990	The batch's own self-check confirmed that triangle counts increased while every
1991	file it wrote was unusable.
1992	
1993	**Re-imported STEPs survive as ground truth.** Fusion can heal or reparameterize
1994	geometry on STEP import, which would quietly destroy the pairing. Measured over
1995	15 exports: vertices lie on the *original* reference surfaces at 4.1e-7 to
1996	7.9e-6 mm, so the verification rate for that source is 15/15. That is the first
1997	field application of the 0.5c gate, and it says a commercial CAD round-trip is
1998	safe to treat as ground truth — which matters for 0.5b, where wild pairs are
1999	expected to fail the same gate for unrelated reasons (different revision,
2000	decimated, repaired).
2001	
2002	**The provenance premise survives contact with the wild — but a third of
2003	published pairs are not usable ground truth.** Of 18 STEP+STL pairs harvested
2004	from open-source hardware repos (0.5b), 11 correspond and 7 verify outright; 5
2005	describe different objects and 2 have meshes too broken to validate. Where they
2006	correspond the premise holds hard: the *median* vertex sits on the CAD surface at
2007	the float32 quantum, and 4 of the 7 that pass do so at or below one quantum, on
2008	files we did not export.
2009	
2010	*The first pass of this reported 6 of 11, and both numbers were wrong.* It
2011	deleted 7 pairs as "different objects" on the strength of \`BRepBndLib::Add(shape,
2012	box, useTriangulation = true)\` — where the flag means "use a triangulation IF THE
2013	FACE HAS ONE", and a shape straight from STEP has none, so OCCT returned bounds
2014	enclosing the surface poles. On a 30 mm cube that box reads 34.8 mm on one axis.
2015	Two compounding errors followed: a padded reference box compared against an exact
2016	mesh box invented size differences, and the denominator shrank because the
2017	failures had been deleted. One of the deleted pairs verifies at 4.5e-5 mm. **Mesh
2018	a shape before you bound it, keep the URLs and hashes of anything you delete, and
2019	never quote a rate over a corpus you pruned using the same measurement.** Four
2020	durable lessons:
2021	
2022	*A max is the wrong statistic for a wild mesh.* Both ToolChanger ducts are exact
2023	to float32 at p95 (6.9e-6 mm against a 9.3e-6 bound) and fail only on a thin tail
2024	reaching 4.3e-4. A single stray vertex — from repair, decimation or a
2025	simplified small feature — is a different fact about a mesh than a systematic
2026	offset, and the max cannot distinguish them. Stage 1's compare therefore reports
2027	**p50 / p95 / max**; the shape of the distribution names the cause on sight.
2028	
2029	*Rigid alignment cannot be more precise than the mesh's own sag — so below that
2030	it must not act at all.* A tessellated mesh's bounding box is inset from the true
2031	solid, and asymmetrically, so a transform recovered from bbox centres carries a
2032	sag-scale error. Applying such a transform where none was needed makes a good
2033	pair worse: two ToolChanger ducts went from a vertex p95 of 6.9e-6 mm to 4.4e-4
2034	under a 4.4e-4 mm "correction". Alignment therefore ignores translations below a
2035	noise floor, and the floor is a measured gap rather than a tuned constant — the
2036	corpus's shifts are bimodal with 4000× of empty space between the noise (8e-8 to
2037	1.6e-3 mm) and the real displacements (6.5 mm and up), so any cut inside it gives
2038	the same answer. The skipped magnitude is printed, so a corpus that lands in the
2039	gap announces itself. Above the floor the method recovers exact round-number
2040	placements perfectly (\`NucDeck\`, 6.52 mm → 4.58e-7 mm) and nothing finer. Going
2041	below would mean fitting the transform to the distance being tested, which is why
2042	alignment stays discrete: bbox centres plus the first of the 24 proper rotations
2043	of a cube whose unsorted extents match. It never sees the distance it helps to
2044	pass, so it cannot make a wrong pair look right.
2045	
2046	*Check the file's text precision before theorizing about geometry.* An ASCII STL
2047	at 6 significant digits has ~1e-4 mm resolution on a 50 mm part, which is exactly
2048	the scale of these residuals — a coincidence worth ruling out rather than
2049	assuming. Here it was ruled out: the STEPs carry 15–20 significant digits and the
2050	STLs are binary.
2051	
2052	**The cliff count is 9 of 32, and four of the nine are the blind patch.** The
2053	fragility meter (\`tools/fragility.sh\`) perturbs one constant at a time by ±2×
2054	and reports which models change and which *collapse* — a passing model that
2055	now fails, or a surface count that at least doubles. \`MAX_PATCH_DEPTH\`,
2056	\`MIN_CONDITION_FACES\`, \`PATCH_TRIM_ROUNDS\` and \`SEED_PATCH_CAP\` are cliffs and
2057	account for most of the collapses; all four are already on Phase 1.5's deletion
2058	list. The thesis above — "one component, blind-patch seeding, forces most of the
2059	guard pile" — was inferred from single-model debugging sessions; it is now a
2060	measurement over 45 models, and Phase 1's success criterion can be stated as
2061	*those four cliffs disappear without new ones appearing*.
2062	
2063	Three things the meter found that were not part of the thesis:
2064	
2065	- **\`NORMAL_AXIS_TOL\` (conical.rs, 0.05) is a cliff nobody had flagged**:
2066	  halving it collapses all three \`rand_cone_chain\` models,
2067	  \`block_with_conical_hole\` and the OCCT re-mesh of \`cone_tangent_planes\`. It
2068	  appears on no deletion list and no watch list. Any future work on cone axes
2069	  should treat it as both load-bearing and fragile.
2070	- **\`MIN_HALF_ANGLE_DEG\` and \`PROJ_GAP_RECHECK\` are cliffs upward only**
2071	  (2° → 4° kills the random cone chains, whose half-angles are genuinely small;
2072	  0.02 → 0.04 collapses a cone chain and a filleted box). Cliffs are not
2073	  symmetric, so a meter that only tightened or only loosened would have missed
2074	  both.
2075	- **16 of 32 constants are completely insensitive to ±2×.** This is *not* a
2076	  deletion list, and reading it as one would be the same mistake as
2077	  complexity-pricing: a guard that fires far from its own boundary is exactly
2078	  what a correct guard looks like. \`MIN_AXIAL_SWEEP_DEG\` is measured essential
2079	  (it is what stops a surface of revolution being fitted by support that never
2080	  observes the revolution) *and* measured ±2×-insensitive. Phase 4.2's
2081	  ablations must therefore disable, not perturb — the two experiments answer
2082	  different questions, and the meter answers only "is this value
2083	  load-bearing".
2084	
2085	Cost and cadence, since the full matrix costs ~110 minutes: run it at phase
2086	boundaries, not per commit, and use \`--only\` for the constants a change plausibly
2087	touches. The current implementation is about 3× slower than necessary — it
2088	parallelizes models within one perturbation's sweep, where wall time is pinned by
2089	the single 101 s model at 39% core utilization, when it should parallelize across
2090	perturbations. Phase 2.1's runtime work on that model removes the binding
2091	constraint either way.
2092	
2093	**The f32 "quantum" is the wrong quantity for a distance, as recorded — and
2094	here is the right one.** The bound on how far f32 storage can move a vertex off
2095	its true surface is \`max over vertices of ‖(ulpₓ, ulp_y, ulp_z)‖ / 2\`: each
2096	coordinate's error is at most half an ulp, and the largest projection of that
2097	box onto any unit direction is the norm of its half-widths (Cauchy–Schwarz).
2098	Measured on the 1 m off-origin meshes: the bound is 4.32e-5 mm and the true max
2099	vertex-to-surface distance is 3.93e-5 mm, so the bound is real and tight (0.91),
2100	while \`max|coordinate| × 1.19e-7\` claims 6.10e-5 mm — 1.4× too loose, because
2101	it ignores that a distance is a projection while also ignoring that all three
2102	coordinates contribute. Both figures are printed in the stage-1 summary (the
2103	naive one for continuity with earlier notes); only the bound is used as a
2104	floor. **The clamp is inert across the whole corpus**, including at 1 m, and
2105	first bites past ~1.02 m where the f32 exponent steps. It is a correctness
2106	floor for a part positioned in a large assembly, not a lever on corpus scores.
2107	
2108	### What a tessellator actually bounds (measured 2026-07-30)
2109	
2110	\`--surface-tolerance\` and the exporter's chordal setting are **the same physical
2111	quantity**, so the tolerance is not merely guessable — it is recoverable. Every
2112	tessellator driven so far takes *two* bounds, not one: OCCT's
2113	\`IncrementalMesh(shape, deflection, …, angle, …)\` and Fusion's \`surfaceDeviation\`
2114	plus \`normalDeviation\`. Both subdivide until both bounds hold, so
```

> I'm looking at how stage 1 implements the `--compare` flag for distribution metrics like p50/p95/max, since that's the pattern for wiring diagnostics into brepper as a comparison feature. I need to check stage1.rs and config.rs to understand how this works, then figure out if the provenance measurement should follow the same approach as a per-stage compare option.
> 
> The core measurement is cylinder-axis error from the cross product of two facet normals, which requires two things: verifying each facet normal is perpendicular to the cylinder axis, and checking if the two-facet cross product recovers that axis. Rather than a one-off probe, I could extend the existing `--compare` infrastructure to associate every mesh face with its true reference surface using the b_rep_adaptor, which would give me ground-truth labeling of the mesh and let me measure normal error against the true surface directly.

/// and maximum angular deviation between adjacent STL faces on the same STEP surface.
///
/// Reads an STL file to extract vertex positions and triangle centroids, reads a
/// STEP file to extract surfaces, and computes the minimum distance from each

/// triangle to its nearest STEP surface, then measures the dihedral angle
/// between adjacent triangles sharing the same STEP surface.
///
/// Units: all distances are in mm. OCCT's STEPControl_Reader converts STEP

    // Compute STL surface area and volume

    let mut stl_area = 0.0_f64;

        // Triangle area = 0.5 * |cross(p2-p1, p3-p1)|

        stl_volume += (p1.x() * cross_x + p1.y() * cross_y + p1.z() * cross_z) / 6.0;

    let mut tri_centroids: Vec<[f64; 3]> = Vec::with_capacity(num_triangles as usize);

    for i in 1..=num_triangles {
        let triangle = tri.triangle(i);
        let mut n1 = 0_i32;
        let mut n2 = 0_i32;
        let mut n3 = 0_i32;
        triangle.get(&mut n1, &mut n2, &mut n3);

        let p1 = tri.node(n1);
        let p2 = tri.node(n2);
        let p3 = tri.node(n3);

        // Centroid
        let cx = (p1.x() + p2.x() + p3.x()) / 3.0;
        let cy = (p1.y() + p2.y() + p3.y()) / 3.0;
        let cz = (p1.z() + p2.z() + p3.z()) / 3.0;
        tri_centroids.push([cx, cy, cz]);

        // Face normal via cross product
        let e1 = [p2.x() - p1.x(), p2.y() - p1.y(), p2.z() - p1.z()];
        let e2 = [p3.x() - p1.x(), p3.y() - p1.y(), p3.z() - p1.z()];
        let nx = e1[1] * e2[2] - e1[2] * e2[1];
        let ny = e1[2] * e2[0] - e1[0] * e2[2];
        let nz = e1[0] * e2[1] - e1[1] * e2[0];
        let len = (nx * nx + ny * ny + nz * nz).sqrt();
        if len > 1e-30 {
            tri_normals.push([nx / len, ny / len, nz / len]);
        } else {
            tri_normals.push([0.0, 0.0, 0.0]); // degenerate
        }

        // Register edges (sorted node indices)
        let edges = [(n1, n2), (n2, n3), (n1, n3)];
        for (a, b) in edges {
            let key = (a.min(b), a.max(b));
            edge_to_triangles.entry(key).or_default().push(i);
        }
    }

    // Map each triangle to its nearest STEP surface using GeomAPI_ProjectPointOnSurf
    let mut tri_surface_idx: Vec<usize> = Vec::with_capacity(num_triangles as usize);
    use opencascade_sys::geom_api;
    for tc in &tri_centroids {
        let pt = gp::Pnt::new_real3(tc[0], tc[1], tc[2]);
        let mut best_face = 0_usize;
        let mut best_dist = f64::MAX;
        for (fi, surface) in step_surfaces.iter().enumerate() {
            let projector = geom_api::ProjectPointOnSurf::new_pnt_handlegeomsurface_extalgo(
                &pt, surface, extrema::ExtAlgo::Grad,
            );
            if projector.is_done() && projector.nb_points() > 0 {
                let d = projector.lower_distance();
                if d < best_dist {
                    best_dist = d;
                    best_face = fi;
                }
            }
        }
        tri_surface_idx.push(best_face);
    }

    // For adjacent triangle pairs on the same STEP surface, compute dihedral angle
    let mut same_surface_angles: Vec<f64> = Vec::new();
    for tris in edge_to_triangles.values() {
        if tris.len() == 2 {
            let t1 = (tris[0] - 1) as usize; // convert 1-based to 0-based
            let t2 = (tris[1] - 1) as usize;
            if tri_surface_idx[t1] == tri_surface_idx[t2] {
                let n1 = &tri_normals[t1];
                let n2 = &tri_normals[t2];
                // Skip degenerate triangles
                let len1 = n1[0] * n1[0] + n1[1] * n1[1] + n1[2] * n1[2];
                let len2 = n2[0] * n2[0] + n2[1] * n2[1] + n2[2] * n2[2];
                if len1 < 0.5 || len2 < 0.5 {
                    continue;
                }
                let dot = n1[0] * n2[0] + n1[1] * n2[1] + n1[2] * n2[2];
                let angle_rad = dot.clamp(-1.0, 1.0).acos();
                let angle_deg = angle_rad.to_degrees();
                same_surface_angles.push(angle_deg);
            }
        }
    }

    let (ang_max, ang_avg, ang_count) = if same_surface_angles.is_empty() {
        (0.0_f64, 0.0_f64, 0_usize)
    } else {
        let max = same_surface_angles.iter().cloned().fold(0.0_f64, f64::max);
        let avg = same_surface_angles.iter().sum::<f64>() / same_surface_angles.len() as f64;
        (max, avg, same_surface_angles.len())
    };

    let vtx_avg_dist = vtx_dist_sum / num_nodes as f64;
    let ctr_avg_dist = if ctr_count > 0 {
        ctr_dist_sum / ctr_count as f64
    } else {
        0.0
    };

    let worst_pt = tri.node(vtx_max_idx);
    eprintln!(
        "Worst vertex #{}: ({:.6}, {:.6}, {:.6})",
        vtx_max_idx,
        worst_pt.x(),
        worst_pt.y(),
        worst_pt.z()
    );
    eprintln!("Vertex   avg: {:.10} mm  max: {:.10} mm", vtx_avg_dist, vtx_max_dist);
    eprintln!("Centroid avg: {:.10} mm  max: {:.10} mm", ctr_avg_dist, ctr_max_dist);
    eprintln!(
        "Angular  avg: {:.4} deg  max: {:.4} deg  ({} same-surface edges)",
        ang_avg, ang_max, ang_count
    );

    // Print tab-separated values to stdout for scripting:
    // vtx_max  vtx_avg  ctr_max  ctr_avg  max_dimension  ang_max  ang_avg  step_area  step_vol  stl_area  stl_vol
    println!(
        "{:.6e}\t{:.6e}\t{:.6e}\t{:.6e}\t{:.6}\t{:.4}\t{:.4}\t{:.4}\t{:.4}\t{:.4}\t{:.4}",
        vtx_max_dist, vtx_avg_dist, ctr_max_dist, ctr_avg_dist, max_dimension, ang_max, ang_avg,
        step_area, step_volume, stl_area, stl_volume
    );
}
```
</details>

> I'm seeing that the existing `step_measure_tolerance.rs` tool already does most of what I was planning to build — it reads both STL and STEP files, projects each triangle onto the nearest STEP surface using geometric projection, and then measures dihedral angles between adjacent triangles that belong to the same surface.
> 
> The gaps for Phase 1.1 are that it doesn't extract surface type information (cylinder axis, cone parameters, etc.) from the STEP geometry, doesn't compare actual triangle normals against the true surface normals, and doesn't measure the n₁×n₂ initializer. There's also a scaling concern: projecting 225K triangles across dozens of faces could be slow, though our test models are small enough that it's manageable. Plus it's a standalone binary that re-reads the STL rather than integrating with the pipeline's existing mesh representation, and it duplicates some measurements that the compare stage already handles better.
> 
> I'm noticing a critical bug: `step_measure_tolerance` uses `DistShapeShape` to measure distances against the solid itself, which is exactly the issue fixed in the pipeline on 2026-07-29 where interior vertices incorrectly read distance 0. The centroid measurement has the same problem — it tessellates, computes centroids that fall inside the solid, and gets vacuous 0 distances. This is the exact bug documented in the development plan. I need to check if this tool is actually used anywhere or if its numbers are being quoted in reports.
> 
> Now for the user's actual question about wiring the provenance probe into brepper as a comparison flag — yes, that makes sense. The right approach is to extend the existing reference labeling system in the binary, but place it where `--compare` lives so it can use the pipeline's fixed mesh and corrected distance helpers. I'm thinking through the design options: one approach would be a new diagnostic mode on the main brepper binary that takes a reference step file and labels every mesh face with its nearest reference face, surface type, and parameters after the first stage completes.
> 
> The key metric here is per-provenance normal error — the angle between a facet's normal and the true surface normal at that facet's projected centroid. This single measurement answers the cylinder axis question, the sphere circumcenter question, and the torus meridian question all at once. For cylinder-labeled faces specifically, I'd report the direct angle deviation from 90° and the normal pair error across adjacent same-label faces.
> 
> The other option is extending `step_measure_tolerance`, which already does the association and adjacency work needed, though there are some tradeoffs to consider.
> 
> I'm also thinking about `dump_regions` — it's the per-face diagnostic tool, so I should check if it supports comparison mode and where it's invoked from in the scripts. And the ground-truth labeling actually gives us the over-segmentation metric for free by counting distinct reference faces covered and comparing our surface partition against the reference partition, which is essentially a segmentation agreement measure.
> 
> The key question is whether this over-segmentation check belongs as its own proper step rather than a throwaway diagnostic, especially since there's a bug to report. Let me verify a few things: whether `step_measure_tolerance` is actually used by any existing scripts, what `dump_regions` does structurally, and how expensive the triangle-to-face association computation would be.
