```
//! Debug dump for stage 2 (scratch tool, not shipped).
//!
//! \`dump_regions <model.stl> <mode> [args]\`
//!
//! Stage 2.1/2.2 segmentation: \`summary\`, \`singletons\`, \`adj\`, \`leaks\`,
//! \`rdesc <region>\`, \`geom <face>...\`, \`probe <x> <y> <z> [radius]\`, \`jet
//! <face>\`.
//!
//! Stage 2.3 classification (runs the whole of stage 2):
//! - \`surfaces\` — every committed surface, largest area first, with its
//!   parameters, to line up against a reference STEP's surface list.
//! - \`at <x> <y> <z> [radius]\` — which surfaces own the faces near a point;
//!   for tracing a stage-3 compare failure back to stage 2.
//! - \`owner <face>...\` — the surface owning a face and how well its
//!   neighbours' surfaces explain it (the stage-2.4 relabel criterion).
//! - \`locus [a b]\` — per adjacent surface pair, what the stage-2.4 junction
//!   locus concludes (tangent/transversal, locus built or not, faces on the
//!   wrong side of it); problems first. With two surface indices, the
//!   per-face detail for that one pair.

use brepper::config;
use brepper::stage1::ConnectedMesh;
use brepper::stage2::edges::{classify_edges, compute_jet_field, EdgeClass};
use brepper::stage2::regions::extract_regions;
use brepper::stage1;

fn centroid(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, fi: usize) -> [f64; 3] {
    let f = &mesh.faces[fi];
    let vc = f.vertex_count as usize;
    let mut c = [0.0; 3];
    for &vi in &f.vertex_indices[..vc] {
        let v = &mesh.vertices[vi];
        c[0] += v.x;
        c[1] += v.y;
        c[2] += v.z;
    }
    let n = vc as f64;
    [c[0] / n, c[1] / n, c[2] / n]
}

/// Area of a mesh face (triangle, or a quad as two triangles).
fn tri_area(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, fi: usize) -> f64 {
    let f = &mesh.faces[fi];
    let p = |k: usize| {
        let v = &mesh.vertices[f.vertex_indices[k]];
        [v.x, v.y, v.z]
    };
    let tri = |a: [f64; 3], b: [f64; 3], c: [f64; 3]| {
        let u = [b[0] - a[0], b[1] - a[1], b[2] - a[2]];
        let w = [c[0] - a[0], c[1] - a[1], c[2] - a[2]];
        let x = [u[1] * w[2] - u[2] * w[1], u[2] * w[0] - u[0] * w[2], u[0] * w[1] - u[1] * w[0]];
        0.5 * (x[0] * x[0] + x[1] * x[1] + x[2] * x[2]).sqrt()
    };
    let mut a = tri(p(0), p(1), p(2));
    if f.vertex_count == 4 {
        a += tri(p(0), p(2), p(3));
    }
    a
}
```

> I'm comparing the centroid measurements between two different approaches—the pipeline gives a max of 9.524e-4 mm while the step-measure-tolerance method shows 8.468e-4 mm, which is about a 1.12× difference. The vertex paths match perfectly, so that's consistent, but the centroid discrepancy might be due to how quad fusion is handled between the two methods.

> On cylindrical surfaces, coplanar fusion can occur on strip quads since four vertices spanning two rulings and circumferential positions do lie in the same plane.
```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Scale a binary STL by a factor. Reproduces the documented 1000x-too-small case."""
import struct, sys

src, dst, factor = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2], float(sys.argv[3])
with open(src, "rb") as f:
    header = f.read(80)
    (n,) = struct.unpack("<I", f.read(4))
    out = bytearray(header) + struct.pack("<I", n)
    for _ in range(n):
        vals = struct.unpack("<12f", f.read(48))
        (attr,) = struct.unpack("<H", f.read(2))
        # normal unchanged by uniform scale; scale the 9 vertex coords
        scaled = list(vals[:3]) + [v * factor for v in vals[3:]]
        out += struct.pack("<12f", *scaled) + struct.pack("<H", attr)
with open(dst, "wb") as f:
    f.write(bytes(out))
print(f"wrote {dst}: {n} triangles scaled by {factor}")

```

<details>
<summary>Write private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-sethml-src-brepper/0469f37f-5755-4ff0-85d5-7e448bf7fc14/scratchpad/shrink_stl.py</summary>

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Shrink a binary STL about its own bbox centre, so the result lies strictly
INSIDE the original solid. That is the decisive containment case: a
DistShapeShape-against-a-solid reads 0, a distance-to-boundary reads the gap."""
import struct, sys

src, dst, factor = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2], float(sys.argv[3])
with open(src, "rb") as f:
    blob = f.read()
header, n = blob[:80], struct.unpack("<I", blob[80:84])[0]
tris = []
off = 84
lo = [float("inf")] * 3
hi = [float("-inf")] * 3
for _ in range(n):
    vals = struct.unpack("<12f", blob[off : off + 48])
    attr = struct.unpack("<H", blob[off + 48 : off + 50])[0]
    off += 50
    tris.append((vals, attr))
    for v in range(3):
        for d in range(3):
            c = vals[3 + 3 * v + d]
            lo[d] = min(lo[d], c)
            hi[d] = max(hi[d], c)
mid = [(lo[d] + hi[d]) / 2 for d in range(3)]

out = bytearray(header) + struct.pack("<I", n)
for vals, attr in tris:
    coords = []
    for v in range(3):
        for d in range(3):
            coords.append(mid[d] + (vals[3 + 3 * v + d] - mid[d]) * factor)
    out += struct.pack("<12f", *(list(vals[:3]) + coords)) + struct.pack("<H", attr)
with open(dst, "wb") as f:
    f.write(bytes(out))
print(f"wrote {dst}: {n} tris, shrunk {factor}x about {mid}")

```
</details>

## 🤖💭 Assistant
<!-- msg_011CdZJyQjbtCjKGrGxAFCJF -->

> I found the issue — the solid classifier uses a hard-coded tolerance of 0.001 millimeters when checking if a point is inside the geometry, and when it determines the point is inside, it sets the distance reference to zero.
> 
> The classifier returns `TopAbs_ON` (on the boundary) when a point is within that tolerance of the surface, which triggers the correct distance calculation. But points further inside than 0.001 mm get classified as `TopAbs_IN` and the distance gets forced to exactly zero. This creates a sharp cliff: the tool works correctly for deviations below 1 micrometer and silently fails above it — the worst possible behavior for a precision measurement instrument.
> 
> I can test this threshold directly by shrinking the cube by factors that place the gap just below and just above 0.001 mm. For a 5 mm half-extent, a gap of 0.0005 mm requires a shrink factor of 0.9999, while 0.002 mm requires 0.9996. The first should report a nonzero distance around 5e-4 mm, and the second should read exactly zero. Let me run both to confirm the threshold.

## Verdict: yes — and it's cheaper wired in than standalone, because brepper already computes the labeling and throws it away

**The decisive finding.** Stage 1's `--compare` already runs `DistShapeShape` from every mesh-face centroid to the reference *face compound* ([stage1.rs:1392](src/stage1.rs:1392)). Inside that call OCCT determines **which reference face won** and **at what (u,v)** — and `min_distance_to_shape` ([stage1.rs:1258](src/stage1.rs:1258)) discards both, returning only `value()`. The bindings expose `support_on_shape2(N)` and `par_on_face_s2(N, &mut u, &mut v)`. So per mesh face we can get, for no additional geometric work:

- the reference face it came from → `b_rep_adaptor::Surface` → exact type and parameters (cylinder axis, sphere centre, torus axis/R/r);
- the (u,v) of the closest point → the **exact** surface normal there via `D1`;
- the distance, which is already reported.

That is the entire ground truth the Phase 1.1 probe needs, as a read-back of a computation that already runs on all 158 harness models.

**The pieces exist, in three places, none of them the pipeline:**

| what | where | problem |
|---|---|---|
| triangle → nearest reference surface | [step_measure_tolerance.rs:331](src/bin/step_measure_tolerance.rs:331) | O(tris × ref_faces) projection loop, **13.2 s** on rounded_cube fine vs the pipeline's 1.45 s for the same association; never reads surface *type* |
| reference cylinder/cone/sphere params | [stage2_integration.rs:1879](tests/stage2_integration.rs:1879) | test-only, assert-based, compares *committed hypotheses* not mesh faces |
| correct distance primitives | [compare.rs](src/compare.rs), [stage1.rs:1258](src/stage1.rs:1258) | already `--compare`-gated, already parallel |

**A bug I hit while checking this, which is the argument for one home.** `step-measure-tolerance` still measures against the reference **solid**, so it has the trap [compare.rs](src/compare.rs) documents. It's worse than "reads zero on convex geometry" — OCCT's `SolidTreatment` calls the classifier with a hard-coded `aTolerance = 0.001`, so there's a **cliff at 1 µm**. Measured on a mesh shrunk about its own centre inside its reference:

| gap inside the solid | step-measure-tolerance | pipeline stage 1 |
|---|---|---|
| ~7.2e-4 mm | 7.208063e-4 | 7.208063e-4 (exact agreement) |
| ~2.9e-3 mm | **0.0000000000** | 2.879135e-3 |
| ~2.9 mm | **0.0000000000** | 2.920772e0 |

It is exact where the answer doesn't matter and silently zero where it does. I've left a chip to fix it separately. (This also refines the plan's own account at [DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md:1938](DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md:1938) — the old stage-1 centroid check wasn't vacuous everywhere, only above 1 µm, which is exactly why correct models looked right and nobody noticed.)

**Proposed shape** — a `--compare`-gated diagnostic in the library, off by default, printed like the 0.7 estimators: for each mesh face, label + true normal; then report per surface type the facet-normal error, and per initializer the closed-form axis/centre error against ground truth. It measures the *pipeline's* mesh (post-repair, post-quad-fusion) — the object Phase 1's earn loop actually sees, and the reason `step_measure_tolerance` needed a `--quad` flag bolted on. Phase 1.4 re-runs it as specified, and `tools/sweep.sh` gets provenance coverage across all 158 models for free.

**Two things it unlocks beyond Phase 1.1:**

1. **Reference segmentation scoring** — labels give the true partition, so "9 surfaces against a reference 26 passes every gate" becomes directly measurable. [DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md:1889](DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md:1889) names this as the instrument's known hole and says a fix "belongs in its own step" — Phase 0 is closed, so it can have one.
2. **The untested half of the seam assumption.** 0.5b measured *vertices lie on the surface*; nothing has ever measured *facets don't straddle seams*. The labeling's own ambiguity rate — facets whose vertices disagree on their label — measures it directly, per provenance, and it's the half that's load-bearing for Phase 1's earned patch.

One caveat to design around either way: nearest-face labeling is weakest exactly at junctions, which is where Phase 1 cares most. Requiring all vertices to agree, and reporting the ambiguous count rather than forcing a label, is what makes item 2 a measurement instead of a defect.

The tricky part is mapping the `TopoDS_Shape` returned by `support_on_shape2` back to my reference face list — I'll need to check if there's an `is_same` binding or use `TopTools_IndexedMapOfShape` to maintain the correspondence. To detect ambiguity, I need to label both the centroid and vertices of each mesh face — if they map to different reference faces, that's a sign the facet straddles a seam. The key insight is that a vertex on a seam legitimately belongs to multiple faces, so the real test is whether the facet's interior crosses a reference face boundary, which I can approximate by checking if the centroid and vertices agree on their labels.
> 
> Since vertices on seams are inherently ambiguous by construction, I should instead sample interior points — the centroid plus three points positioned 2/3 of the way from the centroid toward each vertex. If those interior samples disagree with the centroid's label, the facet genuinely straddles two reference faces.
> 
> But there's a subtlety: a facet entirely within one reference face might still report a straddle if a nearby face wraps closer at some sampled point (like at a concave junction). So the metric should be framed as "ambiguity" rather than a definitive straddle — it's a diagnostic flag that's cheap to compute during the centroid pass and off by default.
> 
> For segmentation scoring, I'm comparing the reference partition against our committed surfaces using standard metrics: over-segmentation (how many of our surfaces cover each reference face, ideally 1) and under-segmentation (how many reference faces each of our surfaces spans, ideally 1), both weighted by area since small spurious surfaces matter less.
> 
> I'm settling on a clean reporting format per model with reference face count, our surface count, a split rate measuring fragmentation as the mean number of our surfaces covering ≥1% of each reference face's area, a merge rate for how many reference faces our surfaces span, and label agreement as the fraction of mesh area where our surface's dominant reference face matches the face's own label.
> 
> The ≥1% threshold is just for diagnostics, not a pipeline decision, so I could also report the area-weighted expected number of surfaces per reference face along with purity as the dominant surface's fraction of the reference face area — this avoids arbitrary thresholds while staying standard.
> 
> I'm considering a few threshold-free metrics: area-weighted entropy, Rand-style pair counting, or simpler approaches like fragmentation (the ratio of distinct surface-face pairs to reference faces) and area-weighted purity (the sum of each reference face's dominant surface area divided by total area). I'll report both purity and the raw pair count since they're complementary — purity is robust to stray facets while the pair count is sensitive, and together they tell the full story without needing a tuned constant.

The labeling gets computed once after stage 1 when the mesh is final, but I need stage 2's committed surfaces and their face sets to do the comparison. > `Surface_ResD1` doesn't expose any field accessors, so I can't actually read the surface normal from what `eval_d1` returns. I need to either use OCCT's `GeomLProp_SLProps` class if it's bound, which has a `Normal()` method, or compute the normal analytically from the surface type and parameters—which I'll need anyway for the initializer scoring.
> 
> The analytic approach is cleaner: for planes, cylinders, cones, spheres, and tori, the surface normal at any point is a straightforward closed-form calculation based on the surface parameters and the point location. For B-splines and other types I can't easily parameterize, I'll skip them and mark them as "unlabeled-analytic," which is honest since I'm specifically measuring analytic initializers. I also need to account for OCCT's face orientation flag, which can flip whether the normal points outward or inward, so I'll need to check that when comparing mesh facet normals to the surface normal.

```
//! Tolerance estimators (Phase 0.7): read the exporter's settings back out of
//! the mesh it produced.
//!
//! Two of brepper's three tolerances are guesses. \`--surface-tolerance\` defaults
//! to 0.4 mm against a measured true sag around 1e-3 mm, and
//! \`--angular-tolerance\` stands in for an exporter setting discarded when the
//! STL was written. Both leave evidence in the dihedral angles, because a CAD
//! tessellator subdivides until a chordal bound and an angular bound are both
//! met.
//!
//! **Diagnostics only.** Nothing downstream reads these. Phase 3 decides whether
//! to flip the defaults, on corpus measurements rather than on this printout.
//!
//! # What is reported, and what is deliberately not
//!
//! The **sagitta** estimate is fit-free: for a chord of length \`h\` across an arc
//! turning by \`θ\`, the radius is \`R ≈ h/θ\` and the sagitta is
//! \`R(1 − cos(θ/2)) ≈ Rθ²/8 = hθ/8\`. So \`sag ≈ dihedral × facet size / 8\`, with
//! the dihedral from adjacent facet normals and the facet size from the distance
//! between their centroids.
//!
//! Both quantities are reported as QUANTILES OVER EVERY INTERIOR EDGE, with no
//! attempt to separate smooth edges from sharp ones. That separation was tried
//! and does not survive contact with the corpus:
//!
//! * Otsu's method splits a bimodal distribution, but this one is trimodal —
//!   after quad fusion a flat region still contributes ~0° edges, a curved
//!   surface contributes a cluster at the angular setting, and real edges sit
//!   near 90°. On \`rounded_cube_10_r2_fine\` Otsu split at 1.75°, calling 15534
//!   of 21586 edges sharp on a model that HAS NO SHARP EDGES.
//! * Widest-empty-band fails too: on \`stepped_block_toroidal_fillets_fine\` the
//!   true band is 3°–5° with only 442 of 76443 edges above it, but the sparse
//!   sharp tail from 5° to 103° contains wider empty runs.
//! * Every remaining formulation needed one arbitrary ratio, which is the
//!   "detector needs just one threshold" case TODO.md rule 5 says to stop on.
//!
//! Quantiles sidestep it — but only on a curvature-dominated mesh, and Phase 0.8
//! showed that is a property of our corpus rather than of CAD models. On the
//! synthetic corpus the dihedral distribution is a SINGLE SPIKE at the
//! exporter's angular setting (rounded_cube fine reads p50 2.49° / p95 2.50°;
//! sphere_25 fine 2.50° / 2.50°), because those models are spheres, rounded
//! cubes and fillets by construction. Real print models are mostly flat panels
//! with sharp rims: 3DBenchy reads p50 0.79° / p95 89.21°, OrcaCube 1.13° /
//! 90.00°, xyz-cube 3.00° / 90.00° — **the p95 is a model rim on all three**,
//! and the sag p95 with it (2.0e-2 to 6.2e-1 mm, implausible on inspection).
//!
//! No fixed quantile fixes this: which one lands on the curved mode depends on
//! each model's flat:curved:sharp proportions, so picking one is the "detector
//! needs just one threshold" case again. **So this stays a diagnostic and
//! nothing derives a tolerance from it.** What makes that safe is that the

//! are distinct, and the dihedral quantiles printed beside the sag announce
```

> I see that `MeshFace` already has the normal vector and neighbor indices I need. Now I'm setting up the reference module to handle ground truth labeling from the pipeline's compare call, starting with an enum for different surface types.
> 
> I'm defining the `RefSurface` enum to cover planes, cylinders, cones, spheres, toruses, and a fallback for other geometry types, each with their specific parameters. Then I'm implementing a method to compute the exact unit normal at any point on these surfaces — for a plane it's straightforward, for a cylinder it's the radial direction, and for a cone I need to work out the geometry carefully: given the apex, axis direction, and half-angle, the normal combines a radial component scaled by cosine of the angle with an axial component scaled by negative sine.
> 
> For labeling mesh faces, I'm sampling points at the centroid and interior locations (halfway from centroid to each vertex), then running a distance query to classify each face against the reference geometry.
> 
> The cost scales with the number of samples per face—roughly 5× the current centroid-only approach. For a small model like rounded_cube that's manageable at a few seconds, but for larger meshes like stepped_block it could stretch to around 30 seconds, which is acceptable as an opt-in diagnostic.
> 
> I need to write a wrapper function that handles the distance queries and returns the necessary information...
> 
> Mapping support shapes to indices involves comparing the result against each reference face's shape representation. The tricky part is that the support type can be a Face, Edge, or Vertex—if the closest point lands on an edge of the reference face, that's actually a seam, and the label becomes ambiguous or needs special handling.
> 
> For mesh facets, since they don't cross seams by assumption, their centroids stay inside the trimmed domain. If I see Edge support, that's evidence the point is straddling beyond the trim boundary, which is itself informative. I'll track this as a distinct outcome like `OnEdge` to capture that distinction.
> 
> Now I'm thinking through the cylinder initializer scoring: for pairs of adjacent mesh faces both referencing the same Cylinder, I can estimate the axis by crossing their normals, then measure how far that estimate deviates from the actual cylinder axis. I'll also look at the conditioning of that cross product—how perpendicular the normals are to each other—to understand when the estimate is reliable. For individual facets, I'm tracking perpendicularity to the axis directly: how far each facet normal is from being perpendicular to the cylinder's axis direction.
> 
> For spheres, the approach is similar: each facet gives me a circumcenter and a normal line, and for pairs of adjacent facets I can find where those lines come closest together to estimate the sphere center. I'll report both center error and radius error. Since the mesh fusion stage only combines coplanar triangles, any fused quad will be planar, so I can use standard circumcenter formulas on the triangle subset.
> 
> For the torus, I'm checking whether the meridian coplanarity constraint is actually satisfied by the real facet data. Using the ground-truth axis, I'll compute the residual for each torus-labeled facet and report it both in millimeters and as an angle-like measure to see if the initializer's assumptions hold up.
> 
> The cleanest geometric test is whether the facet normal line passes through the axis — which is equivalent to the coplanarity constraint. I'll compute the line-to-line distance between the normal line and the axis line, normalized as `|(p−a)·(n×d)| / |n×d|` in millimeters, and also guard against degenerate cases where `|n×d|` is near zero.
> 
> Now I'm moving to segmentation scoring, which requires the stage-2 output to evaluate how well the partition and per-face surface assignments match the ground truth.

```
//! Reference labeling: ground truth per mesh face, read back from the compare
//! call the pipeline already makes.
//!
//! **Diagnostics only.** Nothing downstream reads any of this; it is printed
//! under `--reference-report` (which requires `--compare`) and changes no
//! decision. Like [`crate::estimate`], its job is to make a claim measurable
//! before anything is built on it.
//!
//! # What it is for
//!
//! Phase 1 replaces the blind seed patch with a patch grown under validation,
//! initialized in closed form from facet normals — "a planar facet spanning two
//! rulings of a cylinder has its normal exactly perpendicular to the axis, so
//! two facets give the axis exactly". That is exact on strip/quad tessellation
//! and *not* exact on a general Delaunay triangle, and the plan says to measure
//! the error per provenance before relying on it. This module is that
//! measurement, plus the two others that fall out of the same labeling for
//! nearly nothing.
//!
//! # Why it lives here and not in a probe
//!
//! Stage 1's `--compare` already runs `BRepExtrema_DistShapeShape` from every
//! mesh face centroid to a compound of the reference's faces. That call already
//! determines **which reference face won**, and
//! [`crate::stage1::min_distance_to_shape`] throws it away and returns only the
//! distance. `SupportOnShape2` hands it back. So labeling every mesh face with
//! its ground-truth surface — type and exact parameters — costs no additional
//! geometric work, and it runs on the pipeline's own mesh, after repair and
//! quad fusion, which is the mesh Phase 1's earn loop will see. A standalone
//! probe reading raw STL triangles measures a different object:
//! `step_measure_tolerance` needed a `--quad` flag bolted on for exactly that
//! reason.
//!
//! # The three things it reports
//!
//! 1. **Facet-normal exactness**, per reference surface type: the angle between
//!    the mesh facet normal and the exact analytic surface normal. This is the
//!    quantity every closed-form initializer rests on.
//! 2. **Initializer error against ground truth** — cylinder axis from
//!    `n₁ × n₂`, sphere centre from circumcenter normal lines, and the torus
//!    meridian-coplanarity residual — scored per provenance.
//! 3. **The label-ambiguity rate**, which is the untested half of the seam
//!    assumption. Phase 0.5b measured that mesh *vertices* lie on the reference
//!    surfaces; nothing has ever measured that a *facet* does not straddle a
//!    reference face boundary, which is what Design Principle #2 actually
//!    claims and what the earned patch depends on.
//!
//! # Two honest limits, stated because they bound what the numbers mean
//!
//! **Nearest-face labeling is weakest exactly at junctions.** A facet lying
//! wholly inside reference face A can still be nearer to face B at some sample
//! point, where B curves back toward it — near a concave junction especially.
//! So the ambiguity rate is an **upper bound** on facets that truly straddle a
//! seam, never an exact count, and it is reported as such. What makes it useful
//! anyway is the direction of the error: a mesh that respects seams cannot
//! score badly here for any other reason than this bounded one, so a *low* rate
//! is conclusive and a high one is a prompt to look.
//!
//! **Normals are compared unoriented.** An OCCT face carries an orientation
//! flag that flips its natural normal, and a reference read from STEP may
//! disagree with the mesh's own outward convention face by face. Every angle
//! here is therefore the acute one, `min(θ, 180° − θ)`. That is exactly right
//! for what is being asked — perpendicularity to an axis, concurrence of normal
//! lines, coplanarity of a meridian — none of which has a sign.
//!
//! One convenience worth recording: for all five analytic types the surface
//! normal at a sample point equals the normal at that point's footpoint on the
//! surface, because projecting onto the surface moves along the normal and
//! leaves the meridian plane (or the plane's constant normal) unchanged. So the
//! (u,v) of the footpoint is never needed, and no surface evaluation is done at
//! all — the normal comes in closed form from the extracted parameters.

use crate::stage1::ConnectedMesh;
use opencascade_sys::{
    b_rep_adaptor, b_rep_builder_api, b_rep_extrema, extrema, geom_abs, gp, message, top_abs,
    top_exp, topo_ds, OwnedPtr,
};

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Small vector helpers (kept local; this module is diagnostics-only)
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

fn sub(a: [f64; 3], b: [f64; 3]) -> [f64; 3] {
    [a[0] - b[0], a[1] - b[1], a[2] - b[2]]
}

fn add(a: [f64; 3], b: [f64; 3]) -> [f64; 3] {
    [a[0] + b[0], a[1] + b[1], a[2] + b[2]]
}

fn scale(a: [f64; 3], s: f64) -> [f64; 3] {
    [a[0] * s, a[1] * s, a[2] * s]
}

fn dot(a: [f64; 3], b: [f64; 3]) -> f64 {
    a[0] * b[0] + a[1] * b[1] + a[2] * b[2]
}

fn cross(a: [f64; 3], b: [f64; 3]) -> [f64; 3] {
    [
        a[1] * b[2] - a[2] * b[1],
        a[2] * b[0] - a[0] * b[2],
        a[0] * b[1] - a[1] * b[0],
    ]
}

fn norm(a: [f64; 3]) -> f64 {
    dot(a, a).sqrt()
}

/// Unit vector, or `None` if the input is too short to normalize meaningfully.
///
/// The floor is a numeric-conditioning guard, not a tolerance: below it the
/// direction carries no information at all because it is pure rounding noise.
fn unit(a: [f64; 3]) -> Option<[f64; 3]> {
    let n = norm(a);
    if n < 1.0e-30 {
        None
    } else {
        Some(scale(a, 1.0 / n))
    }
}

/// The acute angle between two directions, in degrees. Sign-free by
/// construction — see the module docs on unoriented normals.
fn acute_angle_deg(a: [f64; 3], b: [f64; 3]) -> f64 {
    match (unit(a), unit(b)) {
        (Some(u), Some(v)) => dot(u, v).abs().clamp(0.0, 1.0).acos().to_degrees(),
        _ => f64::NAN,
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Ground truth: the reference's surfaces, as plain data
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// One reference face's surface, with exact parameters read from the STEP.
///
/// Parameters are copied out as plain `f64` rather than held as OCCT handles so
/// the whole ground truth is `Send + Sync` and needs no lifetime tied to the
/// reader.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub enum RefSurface {
    Plane {
        point: [f64; 3],
        normal: [f64; 3],
    },
    Cylinder {
        axis_pos: [f64; 3],
        axis_dir: [f64; 3],
        radius: f64,
    },
    Cone {
        apex: [f64; 3],
        axis_dir: [f64; 3],
        half_angle: f64,
    },
    Sphere {
        centre: [f64; 3],
        radius: f64,
    },
    Torus {
        centre: [f64; 3],
        axis_dir: [f64; 3],
        major: f64,
        minor: f64,
    },
    /// A B-spline, Bezier, revolution, extrusion or offset surface. Carried so
    /// coverage can be reported honestly — a corpus where half the area is
    /// freeform should not look like a corpus where the initializers were
    /// measured on everything.
    Other,
}

impl RefSurface {
    /// A short name for grouping in the report.
    pub fn type_name(&self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            RefSurface::Plane { .. } => "plane",
            RefSurface::Cylinder { .. } => "cylinder",
            RefSurface::Cone { .. } => "cone",
            RefSurface::Sphere { .. } => "sphere",
            RefSurface::Torus { .. } => "torus",
            RefSurface::Other => "other",
        }
    }

    /// The exact unit surface normal at `p`, unoriented.
    ///
    /// Evaluated at `p` itself rather than at its footpoint, which is
    /// equivalent for every analytic type here — see the module docs.
    ///
    /// Returns `None` on the surface's own degeneracies (a point on a
    /// cylinder's axis, a sphere's centre, a cone's apex), where the normal is
    /// genuinely undefined, and for [`RefSurface::Other`].
    pub fn normal_at(&self, p: [f64; 3]) -> Option<[f64; 3]> {
        match *self {
            RefSurface::Plane { normal, .. } => unit(normal),
            RefSurface::Cylinder {
                axis_pos, axis_dir, ..
            } => {
                let d = unit(axis_dir)?;
                let v = sub(p, axis_pos);
                unit(sub(v, scale(d, dot(v, d))))
            }
            RefSurface::Cone {
                apex,
                axis_dir,
                half_angle,
            } => {
                let d = unit(axis_dir)?;
                let v = sub(p, apex);
                let radial = unit(sub(v, scale(d, dot(v, d))))?;
                // Outward normal of a cone opening along +d tilts back toward
                // -d by the half-angle; at half_angle = 0 this is the cylinder
                // case and reduces to the radial direction.
                unit(add(
                    scale(radial, half_angle.cos()),
                    scale(d, -half_angle.sin()),
                ))
            }
            RefSurface::Sphere { centre, .. } => unit(sub(p, centre)),
            RefSurface::Torus {
                centre,
                axis_dir,
                major,
                ..
            } => {
                let d = unit(axis_dir)?;
                let v = sub(p, centre);
                let meridian = unit(sub(v, scale(d, dot(v, d))))?;
                // The centre of the tube circle through p's meridian.
                let tube_centre = add(centre, scale(meridian, major));
                unit(sub(p, tube_centre))
            }
            RefSurface::Other => None,
        }
    }

    /// The surface's axis, for the types that have one.
    pub fn axis(&self) -> Option<([f64; 3], [f64; 3])> {
        match *self {
            RefSurface::Cylinder {
                axis_pos, axis_dir, ..
            } => Some((axis_pos, axis_dir)),
            RefSurface::Cone {
                apex, axis_dir, ..
            } => Some((apex, axis_dir)),
            RefSurface::Torus {
                centre, axis_dir, ..
            } => Some((centre, axis_dir)),
            _ => None,
        }
    }
}

/// Read one reference face's surface parameters.
fn read_surface(face: &topo_ds::Face) -> RefSurface {
    let adaptor = b_rep_adaptor::Surface::new_face(face);
    match adaptor.get_type() {
        geom_abs::SurfaceType::Plane => {
            let pln = adaptor.plane();
            let ax = pln.axis();
            let loc = ax.location();
            let dir = ax.direction();
            RefSurface::Plane {
                point: [loc.x(), loc.y(), loc.z()],
                normal: [dir.x(), dir.y(), dir.z()],
            }
        }
        geom_abs::SurfaceType::Cylinder => {
            let cyl = adaptor.cylinder();
            let ax = cyl.axis();
            let loc = ax.location();
            let dir = ax.direction();
            RefSurface::Cylinder {
                axis_pos: [loc.x(), loc.y(), loc.z()],
                axis_dir: [dir.x(), dir.y(), dir.z()],
                radius: cyl.radius(),
            }
        }
        geom_abs::SurfaceType::Cone => {
            let cone = adaptor.cone();
            let ax = cone.axis();
            let dir = ax.direction();
            let apex = cone.apex();
            RefSurface::Cone {
                apex: [apex.x(), apex.y(), apex.z()],
                axis_dir: [dir.x(), dir.y(), dir.z()],
                half_angle: cone.semi_angle(),
            }
        }
        geom_abs::SurfaceType::Sphere => {
            let sph = adaptor.sphere();
            let c = sph.location();
            RefSurface::Sphere {
                centre: [c.x(), c.y(), c.z()],
                radius: sph.radius(),
            }
        }
        geom_abs::SurfaceType::Torus => {
            let tor = adaptor.torus();
            let ax = tor.axis();
            let loc = ax.location();
            let dir = ax.direction();
            RefSurface::Torus {
                centre: [loc.x(), loc.y(), loc.z()],
                axis_dir: [dir.x(), dir.y(), dir.z()],
                major: tor.major_radius(),
                minor: tor.minor_radius(),
            }
        }
        _ => RefSurface::Other,
    }
}

/// The reference model: a face compound to measure against, plus one
/// [`RefSurface`] per face **in compound-explorer order**.
///
/// The order matters: [`ReferenceModel::support_index`] recovers a face index
/// by walking the same compound with the same explorer, so the two orders are
/// the same by construction rather than by assumption.
pub struct ReferenceModel {
    compound: OwnedPtr<topo_ds::Compound>,
    pub surfaces: Vec<RefSurface>,
}

impl ReferenceModel {
    /// Build the ground truth from a `--compare` shape.
    pub fn load(shape: &topo_ds::Shape) -> Self {
        let compound = crate::compare::face_compound(shape);
        let mut surfaces = Vec::new();
        let mut explorer = top_exp::Explorer::new_shape_shapeenum2(
            compound.as_shape(),
            top_abs::ShapeEnum::Face,
            top_abs::ShapeEnum::Shape,
        );
        while explorer.more() {
            surfaces.push(read_surface(topo_ds::face_shape(explorer.value())));
            explorer.next();
        }
        Self {
            compound,
            surfaces,
        }
    }

    /// The compound of reference faces — the correct distance target. Never
    /// measure against the solid: see [`crate::compare`].
    pub fn shape(&self) -> &topo_ds::Shape {
        self.compound.as_shape()
    }

    pub fn len(&self) -> usize {
        self.surfaces.len()
    }

    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
        self.surfaces.is_empty()
    }

    /// Map a `SupportOnShape2` result back to an index into [`Self::surfaces`].
    ///
    /// Walks the compound and compares with `IsSame`, which is a TShape pointer
    /// comparison — no geometry, so this is cheap next to the distance query it
    /// follows.
    fn support_index(&self, support: &topo_ds::Shape) -> Option<usize> {
        let mut explorer = top_exp::Explorer::new_shape_shapeenum2(
            self.compound.as_shape(),
            top_abs::ShapeEnum::Face,
            top_abs::ShapeEnum::Shape,
        );
        let mut i = 0_usize;
        while explorer.more() {
            if explorer.value().is_same(support) {
                return Some(i);
            }
            explorer.next();
            i += 1;
        }
        None
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Labeling
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Where a sample point's closest approach to the reference landed.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq)]
pub enum Support {
    /// Inside a reference face: an unambiguous label.
    Face(usize),
    /// On a reference edge or vertex. The sample is at a seam, which for a
    /// facet interior point means the facet reaches past a face boundary.
    Seam,
    /// The distance query returned nothing.
    None,
}

/// One mesh face's ground truth.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy)]
pub struct FaceLabel {
    /// The reference face nearest the centroid, if the centroid landed in one.
    pub support: Support,
    /// Distance from the centroid to the reference surface, mm.
    pub distance: f64,
    /// True when the facet's interior sample points do not all agree with the
    /// centroid's label. An **upper bound** on straddling a seam — see the
    /// module docs.
    pub ambiguous: bool,
}

impl FaceLabel {
    /// The reference face index, for faces that got a clean one.
    pub fn face(&self) -> Option<usize> {
        match self.support {
            Support::Face(i) if !self.ambiguous => Some(i),
            _ => None,
        }
    }
}

/// One distance query, returning the winning support as well as the distance.
///
/// This is [`crate::stage1::min_distance_to_shape`] with the support read back
/// instead of discarded — the whole reason this module is cheap.
fn nearest(pt: [f64; 3], model: &ReferenceModel) -> (f64, Support) {
    let p = gp::Pnt::new_real3(pt[0], pt[1], pt[2]);
    let mut vertex = b_rep_builder_api::MakeVertex::new_pnt(&p);
    let vtx_shape = vertex.vertex();
    let progress = message::ProgressRange::new();
    let dist = b_rep_extrema::DistShapeShape::new_shape2_extflag_extalgo_progressrange(
        vtx_shape.as_shape(),
        model.shape(),
        0, // F (unused/obsolete)
        extrema::ExtAlgo::Grad,
        &progress,
    );
    if !dist.is_done() || dist.nb_solution() < 1 {
        return (f64::MAX, Support::None);
    }
    let support = match dist.support_type_shape2(1) {
        b_rep_extrema::SupportType::Isinface => {
            let shape = dist.support_on_shape2(1);
            match model.support_index(&shape) {
                Some(i) => Support::Face(i),
                None => Support::None,
            }
        }
        // The closest point on the reference lies on an edge or a vertex of it,
        // so the sample is off the end of any single face's trimmed domain.
        _ => Support::Seam,
    };
    (dist.value(), support)
}

/// The interior sample points used to test a facet's label for agreement.
///
/// The centroid plus one point halfway from the centroid to each vertex. All
/// are strictly interior, so a facet that lies wholly within one reference face
/// gives the same label at every one of them; a facet reaching across a seam
/// does not. Vertices themselves are deliberately not used: a mesh vertex *on*
/// a seam is expected and says nothing.
fn sample_points(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, fi: usize) -> Vec<[f64; 3]> {
    let face = &mesh.faces[fi];
    let n = face.vertex_count as usize;
    let mut centroid = [0.0_f64; 3];
    for k in 0..n {
        let v = &mesh.vertices[face.vertex_indices[k]];
        centroid[0] += v.x;
        centroid[1] += v.y;
        centroid[2] += v.z;
    }
    let inv = 1.0 / n as f64;
    let centroid = scale(centroid, inv);

    let mut pts = Vec::with_capacity(n + 1);
    pts.push(centroid);
    for k in 0..n {
        let v = &mesh.vertices[face.vertex_indices[k]];
        pts.push([
            0.5 * (centroid[0] + v.x),
            0.5 * (centroid[1] + v.y),
            0.5 * (centroid[2] + v.z),
        ]);
    }
    pts
}

/// Label every mesh face with its ground-truth reference face.
///
/// Parallel across faces using the same pattern and the same safety argument as
/// [`crate::stage1::distances_to_shape`]: `BRepExtrema_DistShapeShape`,
/// `BRepBuilderAPI_MakeVertex`, `TopExp_Explorer` and the `gp` value types have
/// no global mutable state and do not mutate the target shape, so sharing a
/// read-only `&Shape` across threads is sound (LEARNINGS.md, "OCCT Thread
/// Safety").
pub fn label_faces(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, model: &ReferenceModel) -> Vec<FaceLabel> {
    let n = mesh.faces.len();
    let unlabeled = FaceLabel {
        support: Support::None,
        distance: f64::MAX,
        ambiguous: false,
    };
    if n == 0 || model.is_empty() {
        return vec![unlabeled; n];
    }

    let label_one = |fi: usize| -> FaceLabel {
        if mesh.faces[fi].vertex_count == 0 {
            return unlabeled;
        }
        let pts = sample_points(mesh, fi);
        let (distance, support) = nearest(pts[0], model);
        let mut ambiguous = false;
        for &p in &pts[1..] {
            let (_, s) = nearest(p, model);
            if s != support {
                ambiguous = true;
                break;
            }
        }
        FaceLabel {
            support,
            distance,
            ambiguous,
        }
    };

    let threads = std::thread::available_parallelism()
        .map(|p| p.get())
        .unwrap_or(1)
        .min(8)
        .min(n.div_ceil(16).max(1));
    if threads <= 1 {
        return (0..n).map(label_one).collect();
    }

    struct SharedModel(*const ReferenceModel);
    unsafe impl Send for SharedModel {}
    unsafe impl Sync for SharedModel {}
    let shared = SharedModel(model as *const _);

    let chunk = n.div_ceil(threads);
    let mut out = vec![unlabeled; n];
    std::thread::scope(|s| {
        for (ci, out_chunk) in out.chunks_mut(chunk).enumerate() {
            let shared = &shared;
            s.spawn(move || {
                let model = unsafe { &*shared.0 };
                let base = ci * chunk;
                for (k, slot) in out_chunk.iter_mut().enumerate() {
                    let fi = base + k;
                    if mesh.faces[fi].vertex_count == 0 {
                        continue;
                    }
                    let pts = sample_points(mesh, fi);
                    let (distance, support) = nearest(pts[0], model);
                    let mut ambiguous = false;
                    for &p in &pts[1..] {
                        let (_, s) = nearest(p, model);
                        if s != support {
                            ambiguous = true;
                            break;
                        }
                    }
                    *slot = FaceLabel {
                        support,
                        distance,
                        ambiguous,
                    };
                }
            });
        }
    });
    out
}
```

> The first task compiled successfully, so now I'm moving on to implement the reporting functionality. I need to define the Report struct with fields for tracking reference faces, labeled faces, ambiguous cases, and seam information.
> 
> I'm also adding fields to capture area metrics and normal error quantiles by surface type, plus optional scores for cylinders, spheres, toruses, and segmentation results. I'll need a quantiles helper function following the existing pattern from stage1, then implement the normal report by computing angle errors between facet normals and analytic surface normals grouped by type, and build the cylinder initializer to evaluate adjacent face pairs with the same cylinder label by measuring axis alignment and normal conditioning.
> 
> For cylinder pairs, I'm computing perpendicularity error as the deviation from 90 degrees between each facet normal and the axis direction, then reporting perpendicularity and pair-axis error quantiles alongside conditioning metrics—possibly binning the results by conditioning level to show how error varies with pair quality.
> 
> For sphere initialization, I'm extracting the circumcenter of each facet and using the normal line through it to estimate the sphere's center, then measuring both the center error and radius error in millimeters, plus checking how far the true center lies from each facet's normal line.
> 
> For the torus case, I'm computing the line-line distance between each facet's normal line and the true axis, reporting the distance in millimeters along with percentiles and conditioning metrics to assess how well the normal geometry aligns with the actual torus axis.
> 
> For segmentation, I'm calculating the area of each mesh face and then for every pair of our surfaces and reference faces, accumulating the overlapping area to compute purity as the area-weighted ratio of maximum overlap per reference face divided by total area.  I'm also computing inverse purity the same way but per our surface, then counting distinct pairs to compare against the reference and our surface counts — perfect segmentation would have all three equal with both purity metrics at 1, while over-segmentation (splitting reference faces across multiple our surfaces) would lower purity even if each of our surfaces maps cleanly to one reference face. single reference face (no under-segmentation). This is threshold-free and clean. Now I'll implement these metrics in the reference module and check how the quantile calculation works in the estimate module first.

```bash
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Distributions
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// p50 / p95 / max of a sample, with its size. The shape of a distribution
/// names the cause on sight where a max alone cannot — the lesson from the
/// wild-pair corpus in Phase 0.5b, applied here too.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Default)]
pub struct Dist {
    pub p50: f64,
    pub p95: f64,
    pub max: f64,
    pub n: usize,
}

impl Dist {
    fn of(mut v: Vec<f64>) -> Self {
        v.retain(|x| x.is_finite());
        if v.is_empty() {
            return Self {
                p50: f64::NAN,
                p95: f64::NAN,
                max: f64::NAN,
                n: 0,
            };
        }
        v.sort_by(f64::total_cmp);
        let at = |q: f64| v[(((v.len() - 1) as f64) * q).round() as usize];
        Self {
            p50: at(0.50),
            p95: at(0.95),
            max: at(1.0),
            n: v.len(),
        }
    }

    fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
        self.n == 0
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Geometry the initializers are built from
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Area of a mesh face (a quad as two triangles).
fn face_area(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, fi: usize) -> f64 {
    let f = &mesh.faces[fi];
    let p = |k: usize| {
        let v = &mesh.vertices[f.vertex_indices[k]];
        [v.x, v.y, v.z]
    };
    let tri = |a: [f64; 3], b: [f64; 3], c: [f64; 3]| 0.5 * norm(cross(sub(b, a), sub(c, a)));
    let mut area = tri(p(0), p(1), p(2));
    if f.vertex_count == 4 {
        area += tri(p(0), p(2), p(3));
    }
    area
}

fn face_centroid(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, fi: usize) -> [f64; 3] {
    let f = &mesh.faces[fi];
    let n = f.vertex_count as usize;
    let mut c = [0.0_f64; 3];
    for k in 0..n {
        let v = &mesh.vertices[f.vertex_indices[k]];
        c = add(c, [v.x, v.y, v.z]);
    }
    scale(c, 1.0 / n as f64)
}

/// The circumcenter of a facet's first three vertices.
///
/// For a facet inscribed in a sphere this is the point whose normal line passes
/// **exactly** through the centre, which is what makes the sphere initializer
/// closed-form. A quad here is planar by construction (stage 1 fuses only
/// coplanar triangles), so its four vertices are concyclic and any three give
/// the same circumcenter.
fn circumcenter(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, fi: usize) -> Option<[f64; 3]> {
    let f = &mesh.faces[fi];
    if (f.vertex_count as usize) < 3 {
        return None;
    }
    let p = |k: usize| {
        let v = &mesh.vertices[f.vertex_indices[k]];
        [v.x, v.y, v.z]
    };
    let (a, b, c) = (p(0), p(1), p(2));
    let ab = sub(b, a);
    let ac = sub(c, a);
    let n = cross(ab, ac);
    let denom = 2.0 * dot(n, n);
    if denom < 1.0e-30 {
        return None;
    }
    // Standard barycentric form: a + (|ac|² (ab×ac)×ab + |ab|² ac×(ab×ac)) / 2|ab×ac|²
    let term = add(
        scale(cross(n, ab), dot(ac, ac)),
        scale(cross(ac, n), dot(ab, ab)),
    );
    Some(add(a, scale(term, 1.0 / denom)))
}

/// Distance between two lines given as (point, direction).
///
/// Returns `None` when the directions are too nearly parallel for the common
/// perpendicular to be meaningful — a numeric-conditioning guard, not a
/// tolerance.
fn line_line_distance(p1: [f64; 3], d1: [f64; 3], p2: [f64; 3], d2: [f64; 3]) -> Option<f64> {
    let n = cross(d1, d2);
    let len = norm(n);
    if len < 1.0e-12 {
        return None;
    }
    Some((dot(sub(p2, p1), n) / len).abs())
}

/// Midpoint of the common perpendicular between two lines — the standard
/// near-intersection of two normal lines, and the sphere initializer's centre.
fn common_perpendicular_midpoint(
    p1: [f64; 3],
    d1: [f64; 3],
    p2: [f64; 3],
    d2: [f64; 3],
) -> Option<[f64; 3]> {
    let (u, v) = (unit(d1)?, unit(d2)?);
    let w = sub(p1, p2);
    let b = dot(u, v);
    let denom = 1.0 - b * b;
    if denom < 1.0e-12 {
        return None;
    }
    let d = dot(u, w);
    let e = dot(v, w);
    let s = (b * e - d) / denom;
    let t = (e - b * d) / denom;
    let q1 = add(p1, scale(u, s));
    let q2 = add(p2, scale(v, t));
    Some(scale(add(q1, q2), 0.5))
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// The report
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// How exactly facet normals reproduce the true surface normal, by type.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]
pub struct NormalScore {
    /// (type name, angle error in degrees, area on that type)
    pub by_type: Vec<(&'static str, Dist, f64)>,
}

/// The cylinder initializer, scored against ground truth.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]
pub struct CylinderScore {
    /// `|90° − ∠(facet normal, true axis)|`, degrees. The property the
    /// initializer rests on: zero means the normal is exactly perpendicular to
    /// the axis, which is what makes `n₁ × n₂` the axis exactly.
    pub perpendicularity_deg: Dist,
    /// Angle between `normalize(n₁ × n₂)` over an adjacent same-surface pair
    /// and the true axis, degrees.
    pub pair_axis_deg: Dist,
    /// `|n₁ × n₂|` for those pairs — the conditioning of the cross product.
    pub conditioning: Dist,
    /// Median pair-axis error binned by conditioning, worst-conditioned first:
    /// (bin label, count, median error in degrees).
    pub by_conditioning: Vec<(&'static str, usize, f64)>,
}

/// The sphere initializer, scored against ground truth.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]
pub struct SphereScore {
    /// Distance from the true centre to the facet's circumcenter normal line,
    /// mm. Exactly zero for a facet inscribed in the sphere.
    pub normal_line_miss_mm: Dist,
    /// Error of the two-facet centre estimate, mm.
    pub pair_centre_mm: Dist,
    /// Error of the radius implied by that centre, mm.
    pub pair_radius_mm: Dist,
}

/// The torus initializer, scored against ground truth.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]
pub struct TorusScore {
    /// Distance between the facet's normal line and the true axis, mm — the
    /// residual of the meridian-coplanarity constraint
    /// `(pᵢ − a)·(nᵢ × d) = 0` that the axis solve is built on.
    pub meridian_miss_mm: Dist,
    /// `|nᵢ × d|`, the conditioning of that constraint. Near zero where the
    /// normal is nearly parallel to the axis and the row carries no
    /// information.
    pub conditioning: Dist,
}

/// Our stage-2 partition against the reference partition.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]
pub struct SegmentationScore {
    pub ref_faces: usize,
    pub our_surfaces: usize,
    /// Distinct (our surface, reference face) pairs. Equals both counts above
    /// when the partitions agree exactly.
    pub pairs: usize,
    /// Area-weighted: `Σ_R max_S area(R,S) / Σ area`. Below 1 means a reference
    /// face was split across several of our surfaces — **over-segmentation**,
    /// which no existing gate can see.
    pub ref_coverage: f64,
    /// Area-weighted: `Σ_S max_R area(S,R) / Σ area`. Below 1 means one of our
    /// surfaces spans several reference faces — **under-segmentation**.
    pub surf_purity: f64,
}

/// Everything measured against the reference for one model.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]
pub struct Report {
    pub ref_faces: usize,
    pub mesh_faces: usize,
    pub labeled: usize,
    pub ambiguous: usize,
    pub seam: usize,
    pub unlabeled: usize,
    /// Fraction of mesh area whose reference face is an analytic type. The
    /// initializer scores below say nothing about the rest.
    pub analytic_area_fraction: f64,
    pub normals: NormalScore,
    pub cylinder: Option<CylinderScore>,
    pub sphere: Option<SphereScore>,
    pub torus: Option<TorusScore>,
    pub segmentation: Option<SegmentationScore>,
}

/// Score a labeled mesh against its ground truth.
pub fn report(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, model: &ReferenceModel, labels: &[FaceLabel]) -> Report {
    let mut rep = Report {
        ref_faces: model.len(),
        mesh_faces: mesh.faces.iter().filter(|f| f.vertex_count > 0).count(),
        ..Default::default()
    };

    // --- label accounting -------------------------------------------------
    let mut area_total = 0.0_f64;
    let mut area_analytic = 0.0_f64;
    let mut area_by_type: Vec<(&'static str, f64)> = Vec::new();
    let mut normals_by_type: Vec<(&'static str, Vec<f64>)> = Vec::new();

    for (fi, label) in labels.iter().enumerate() {
        if mesh.faces[fi].vertex_count == 0 {
            continue;
        }
        match label.support {
            Support::Seam => rep.seam += 1,
            Support::None => rep.unlabeled += 1,
            Support::Face(_) if label.ambiguous => rep.ambiguous += 1,
            Support::Face(_) => rep.labeled += 1,
        }

        let Some(ri) = label.face() else { continue };
        let surf = &model.surfaces[ri];
        let area = face_area(mesh, fi);
        area_total += area;
        let name = surf.type_name();
        match area_by_type.iter_mut().find(|(n, _)| *n == name) {
            Some(slot) => slot.1 += area,
            None => area_by_type.push((name, area)),
        }
        if matches!(surf, RefSurface::Other) {
            continue;
        }
        area_analytic += area;

        // Facet normal against the exact surface normal at its centroid.
        let (Some(fn_), Some(sn)) = (
            mesh.faces[fi].normal,
            surf.normal_at(face_centroid(mesh, fi)),
        ) else {
            continue;
        };
        let err = acute_angle_deg(fn_, sn);
        match normals_by_type.iter_mut().find(|(n, _)| *n == name) {
            Some(slot) => slot.1.push(err),
            None => normals_by_type.push((name, vec![err])),
        }
    }
    rep.analytic_area_fraction = if area_total > 0.0 {
        area_analytic / area_total
    } else {
        0.0
    };
    rep.normals.by_type = normals_by_type
        .into_iter()
        .map(|(name, errs)| {
            let area = area_by_type
                .iter()
                .find(|(n, _)| *n == name)
                .map(|(_, a)| *a)
                .unwrap_or(0.0);
            (name, Dist::of(errs), area)
        })
        .collect();
    rep.normals
        .by_type
        .sort_by(|a, b| b.2.total_cmp(&a.2));

    // --- initializer scores ----------------------------------------------
    rep.cylinder = score_cylinders(mesh, model, labels);
    rep.sphere = score_spheres(mesh, model, labels);
    rep.torus = score_tori(mesh, model, labels);
    rep
}

/// Adjacent mesh-face pairs that share a clean label, each reported once.
fn same_label_pairs(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, labels: &[FaceLabel]) -> Vec<(usize, usize, usize)> {
    let mut pairs = Vec::new();
    for (fi, face) in mesh.faces.iter().enumerate() {
        let Some(ri) = labels[fi].face() else { continue };
        for k in 0..face.vertex_count as usize {
            let nb = face.neighbors[k];
            if nb < 0 {
                continue;
            }
            let fj = nb as usize;
            if fj <= fi {
                continue; // report each pair once
            }
            if labels[fj].face() == Some(ri) {
                pairs.push((fi, fj, ri));
            }
        }
    }
    pairs
}

fn score_cylinders(
    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
    model: &ReferenceModel,
    labels: &[FaceLabel],
) -> Option<CylinderScore> {
    let mut perp = Vec::new();
    for (fi, label) in labels.iter().enumerate() {
        let Some(ri) = label.face() else { continue };
        let RefSurface::Cylinder { axis_dir, .. } = model.surfaces[ri] else {
            continue;
        };
        let Some(n) = mesh.faces[fi].normal else {
            continue;
        };
        perp.push((90.0 - acute_angle_deg(n, axis_dir)).abs());
    }
    if perp.is_empty() {
        return None;
    }

    let mut pair_err = Vec::new();
    let mut cond = Vec::new();
    let mut binned: Vec<(&'static str, Vec<f64>)> = vec![
        ("|n1xn2| < 0.01", Vec::new()),
        ("0.01 - 0.1", Vec::new()),
        ("0.1 - 0.5", Vec::new()),
        ("> 0.5", Vec::new()),
    ];
    for (fi, fj, ri) in same_label_pairs(mesh, labels) {
        let RefSurface::Cylinder { axis_dir, .. } = model.surfaces[ri] else {
            continue;
        };
        let (Some(n1), Some(n2)) = (mesh.faces[fi].normal, mesh.faces[fj].normal) else {
            continue;
        };
        let c = cross(n1, n2);
        let k = norm(c);
        let Some(est) = unit(c) else { continue };
        let err = acute_angle_deg(est, axis_dir);
        pair_err.push(err);
        cond.push(k);
        // Binning is a presentation choice for a diagnostic — it separates
        // "the normals are wrong" from "the cross product was ill-conditioned",
        // and no pipeline decision reads it.
        let bin = if k < 0.01 {
            0
        } else if k < 0.1 {
            1
        } else if k < 0.5 {
            2
        } else {
            3
        };
        binned[bin].1.push(err);
    }

    Some(CylinderScore {
        perpendicularity_deg: Dist::of(perp),
        pair_axis_deg: Dist::of(pair_err),
        conditioning: Dist::of(cond),
        by_conditioning: binned
            .into_iter()
            .map(|(name, v)| {
                let n = v.len();
                (name, n, Dist::of(v).p50)
            })
            .filter(|(_, n, _)| *n > 0)
            .collect(),
    })
}

fn score_spheres(
    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
    model: &ReferenceModel,
    labels: &[FaceLabel],
) -> Option<SphereScore> {
    let mut miss = Vec::new();
    for (fi, label) in labels.iter().enumerate() {
        let Some(ri) = label.face() else { continue };
        let RefSurface::Sphere { centre, .. } = model.surfaces[ri] else {
            continue;
        };
        let (Some(cc), Some(n)) = (circumcenter(mesh, fi), mesh.faces[fi].normal) else {
            continue;
        };
        // Distance from the true centre to the circumcenter normal line.
        let v = sub(centre, cc);
        let Some(u) = unit(n) else { continue };
        miss.push(norm(sub(v, scale(u, dot(v, u)))));
    }
    if miss.is_empty() {
        return None;
    }

    let mut centre_err = Vec::new();
    let mut radius_err = Vec::new();
    for (fi, fj, ri) in same_label_pairs(mesh, labels) {
        let RefSurface::Sphere { centre, radius } = model.surfaces[ri] else {
            continue;
        };
        let (Some(c1), Some(c2)) = (circumcenter(mesh, fi), circumcenter(mesh, fj)) else {
            continue;
        };
        let (Some(n1), Some(n2)) = (mesh.faces[fi].normal, mesh.faces[fj].normal) else {
            continue;
        };
        let Some(est) = common_perpendicular_midpoint(c1, n1, c2, n2) else {
            continue;
        };
        centre_err.push(norm(sub(est, centre)));
        let r_est = 0.5 * (norm(sub(c1, est)) + norm(sub(c2, est)));
        radius_err.push((r_est - radius).abs());
    }

    Some(SphereScore {
        normal_line_miss_mm: Dist::of(miss),
        pair_centre_mm: Dist::of(centre_err),
        pair_radius_mm: Dist::of(radius_err),
    })
}

fn score_tori(
    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
    model: &ReferenceModel,
    labels: &[FaceLabel],
) -> Option<TorusScore> {
    let mut miss = Vec::new();
    let mut cond = Vec::new();
    for (fi, label) in labels.iter().enumerate() {
        let Some(ri) = label.face() else { continue };
        let RefSurface::Torus {
            centre, axis_dir, ..
        } = model.surfaces[ri]
        else {
            continue;
        };
        let Some(n) = mesh.faces[fi].normal else {
            continue;
        };
        let p = face_centroid(mesh, fi);
        cond.push(norm(cross(n, axis_dir)));
        if let Some(d) = line_line_distance(p, n, centre, axis_dir) {
            miss.push(d);
        }
    }
    if miss.is_empty() {
        return None;
    }
    Some(TorusScore {
        meridian_miss_mm: Dist::of(miss),
        conditioning: Dist::of(cond),
    })
}

/// Score our stage-2 partition against the reference partition.
///
/// `our_partition` is one face-index list per selected surface.
pub fn score_segmentation(
    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
    model: &ReferenceModel,
    labels: &[FaceLabel],
    our_partition: &[Vec<usize>],
) -> SegmentationScore {
    // Area of every (our surface, reference face) overlap.
    let mut overlap: std::collections::HashMap<(usize, usize), f64> =
        std::collections::HashMap::new();
    let mut total = 0.0_f64;
    for (si, faces) in our_partition.iter().enumerate() {
        for &fi in faces {
            let Some(ri) = labels[fi].face() else { continue };
            let a = face_area(mesh, fi);
            *overlap.entry((si, ri)).or_insert(0.0) += a;
            total += a;
        }
    }
    if total <= 0.0 {
        return SegmentationScore {
            ref_faces: model.len(),
            our_surfaces: our_partition.len(),
            ..Default::default()
        };
    }

    let mut best_per_ref: std::collections::HashMap<usize, f64> = std::collections::HashMap::new();
    let mut best_per_surf: std::collections::HashMap<usize, f64> = std::collections::HashMap::new();
    for (&(si, ri), &a) in &overlap {
        let e = best_per_ref.entry(ri).or_insert(0.0);
        *e = e.max(a);
        let e = best_per_surf.entry(si).or_insert(0.0);
        *e = e.max(a);
    }

    SegmentationScore {
        ref_faces: model.len(),
        our_surfaces: our_partition.len(),
        pairs: overlap.len(),
        ref_coverage: best_per_ref.values().sum::<f64>() / total,
        surf_purity: best_per_surf.values().sum::<f64>() / total,
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Printing
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

fn fmt_dist_deg(d: &Dist) -> String {
    if d.is_empty() {
        return "—".to_string();
    }
    format!(
        "p50 {:.4}° p95 {:.4}° max {:.4}° (n={})",
        d.p50, d.p95, d.max, d.n
    )
}

fn fmt_dist_mm(d: &Dist) -> String {
    if d.is_empty() {
        return "—".to_string();
    }
    format!(
        "p50 {:.3e} p95 {:.3e} max {:.3e} mm (n={})",
        d.p50, d.p95, d.max, d.n
    )
}

impl Report {
    /// Print the mesh-side half: labeling, seam assumption, normals,
    /// initializers.
    pub fn print(&self) {
        eprintln!(
            "  Reference: {} faces; labeled {}/{} mesh faces, {} ambiguous, {} at a seam, {} unlabeled",
            self.ref_faces, self.labeled, self.mesh_faces, self.ambiguous, self.seam, self.unlabeled
        );
        let straddle = self.ambiguous + self.seam;
        if self.mesh_faces > 0 {
            eprintln!(
                "    seam assumption: <= {:.4}% of facets may straddle a reference face boundary \
                 (upper bound; {:.1}% of labeled area is analytic)",
                100.0 * straddle as f64 / self.mesh_faces as f64,
                100.0 * self.analytic_area_fraction,
            );
        }
        for (name, dist, area) in &self.normals.by_type {
            eprintln!(
                "    normal error on {name:<9} {}  [{:.1} mm² of mesh]",
                fmt_dist_deg(dist),
                area
            );
        }
        if let Some(c) = &self.cylinder {
            eprintln!(
                "    cylinder  perpendicularity {}",
                fmt_dist_deg(&c.perpendicularity_deg)
            );
            eprintln!(
                "              n1xn2 axis error {}",
                fmt_dist_deg(&c.pair_axis_deg)
            );
            let bins = c
                .by_conditioning
                .iter()
                .map(|(name, n, p50)| format!("{name}: n={n} p50 {p50:.4}°"))
                .collect::<Vec<_>>()
                .join("; ");
            eprintln!("              by conditioning  {bins}");
        }
        if let Some(s) = &self.sphere {
            eprintln!(
                "    sphere    normal-line miss {}",
                fmt_dist_mm(&s.normal_line_miss_mm)
            );
            eprintln!(
                "              pair centre err  {}",
                fmt_dist_mm(&s.pair_centre_mm)
            );
        }
        if let Some(t) = &self.torus {
            eprintln!(
                "    torus     meridian residual {}",
                fmt_dist_mm(&t.meridian_miss_mm)
            );
            eprintln!(
                "              |n x d|          {}",
                fmt_dist_mm(&t.conditioning)
            );
        }
    }
}

impl SegmentationScore {
    pub fn print(&self) {
        eprintln!(
            "  Segmentation vs reference: {} reference faces, {} of ours, {} overlapping pairs",
            self.ref_faces, self.our_surfaces, self.pairs
        );
        eprintln!(
            "    ref coverage {:.4} (1 = no over-segmentation), surface purity {:.4} (1 = no merging)",
            self.ref_coverage, self.surf_purity
        );
    }
}
```

```
impl std::fmt::Debug for Config {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
        f.debug_struct("Config")
            .field("input_stl", &self.input_stl)
            .field("output_step", &self.output_step)
            .field("stl_units", &self.stl_units)
            .field("step_units", &self.step_units)
            .field("compare_step", &self.compare_step)
            .field("compare_shape", &if self.compare_shape.is_some() { format!("[{} faces]", self.compare_face_count) } else { "None".to_string() })
            .field("vertex_tolerance_mm", &self.vertex_tolerance_mm)
            .field("surface_tolerance_mm", &self.surface_tolerance_mm)
            .field("angular_tolerance_rad", &self.angular_tolerance_rad)
            .field("verbosity", &self.verbosity)
            .field("verbose", &self.verbose)
            .field("quiet", &self.quiet)
            .field("strict_mesh", &self.strict_mesh)
            .field("debug", &self.debug)
            .field("stage", &self.stage)
            .field("viz_stages", &self.viz_stages)
            .field("viz_stages_level", &self.viz_stages_level)
            .finish()
    }
}

impl Config {
    fn from_cli(args: CliArgs) -> Self {
        let scale = args.stl_units.to_mm();
        Self {
            input_stl: args.input_stl,
            output_step: args.output_step,
            stl_units: args.stl_units,
            step_units: args.step_units,
            compare_step: args.compare_step,
            compare_shape: None,
            compare_face_count: 0,
            vertex_tolerance_mm: args.vertex_tolerance * scale,
            surface_tolerance_mm: args.surface_tolerance * scale,
            angular_tolerance_rad: args.angular_tolerance.to_radians(),
            verbosity: args.verbose_level.unwrap_or(0).max(args.verbose_short),
            verbose: args.verbose_level.unwrap_or(0).max(args.verbose_short) >= 1,
            quiet: args.quiet,
            strict_mesh: args.strict_mesh,
            debug: args.debug,
            stage: args.stage.unwrap_or_default(),
            viz_stages: args.viz_stages.unwrap_or_default(),
            viz_stages_level: args.viz_stages_level,
        }
    }

    /// Check if a specific stage should be visualized.
    pub fn viz_active(&self, major: u8, minor: u8) -> bool {
        self.viz_stages.iter().any(|s| {
            let (sm, ss) = s.normalized();
            sm == major && ss == minor
        })
    }
```

## 🤖💭 Assistant
<!-- msg_011CdZLUAQZa6kj3grzxBVtu -->

> The test results are validating the core assumptions—the seam detection shows perfect labeling on the Parasolid mesh with no ambiguous cases, and the cylinder perpendicularity measurements confirm that facet normals align almost exactly perpendicular to the axis, which supports the Phase 1.1 hypothesis.
> 
> The n₁×n₂ axis error reveals a clear conditioning pattern: pairs with very small cross products (|n₁×n₂| < 0.01) show much larger errors around 0.61°, while better-conditioned pairs stay near zero, so this is a numerical stability issue rather than a problem with the normals themselves. The sphere measurements show small normal-line misses but some outlier pair centre errors up to 4 mm, again driven by nearly-parallel normal lines. I'm also noticing something curious about the geometry fusion—the rounded cube appears to have merged its flat faces into single quads, so a 10mm square face that was originally two triangles became one quad, which explains why the sphere has far more facets but less area than the cylinder. So the spherical corners are staying as individual triangles rather than fusing into quads because they're non-coplanar, which explains why there are so many more of them — 1293 triangles per corner for an 8-corner rounded cube seems excessive for a 2mm radius sphere though, even accounting for the 2.5° angular tolerance.
> 
> Let me verify this makes sense: a full sphere at that tolerance would have roughly 10,368 triangles, so each octant should be around 1,300 — and that's exactly what I'm seeing with 8 octants × 1,293 = 10,344. The cylinder strips are also checking out: 36 rows around the circumference at 2.5° intervals, with minimal lengthwise division since cylinders are ruled surfaces, giving about 432 quads which aligns with the observed 504 facets.
> 
> The labeling is consistent then, though the 4.2-second runtime is noticeably slower than the 1.45 seconds for a plain comparison — still acceptable, but worth noting.
> 
> Now I'm checking the conditioning distribution across the adjacent pairs: with 20,170 pairs versus 10,343 sphere facets, that's roughly 1.5× the face count, which makes sense for adjacency. The key insight is that no pairs exceed 0.1 conditioning because adjacent facets on a cylinder differ by only 2.5°, giving |n₁·n₂| = sin(2.5°) ≈ 0.0436 — so everything clusters in the 0.01–0.1 bin, with just 20 pairs below 0.01 (likely coplanar facets along the cylinder axis). This validates the Phase 1.1 adjacent-pair conditioning distribution.
> 
> The axis is still recovered to 0.0000° despite the inherent ~0.04 conditioning on the fine mesh. Now I need to wire up the segmentation half at stage 2, add unit tests, and measure across different provenances. Let me verify the Report::print is capturing everything — the torus section correctly didn't appear since there are no tori in this model. I should also investigate that 4.079 mm pair centre error on a 2 mm sphere, which is the ill-conditioned pair case, so I'll add conditioning breakdown reporting for the sphere as well.
> 
> Adding sphere pair conditioning...

```
impl Stage2Output {
    /// The mesh faces owned by each selected surface, in `selected_surfaces`
    /// order — our partition of the mesh.
    pub fn selected_face_sets(&self) -> Vec<Vec<usize>> {
        self.selected_surfaces
            .iter()
            .map(|s| match s {
                SelectedSurface::Planar(i) => self.planar_hypotheses[*i].faces.clone(),
                SelectedSurface::Conical(i) => self.conical_hypotheses[*i].faces.clone(),
                SelectedSurface::Spherical(i) => self.spherical_hypotheses[*i].faces.clone(),
                SelectedSurface::Toroidal(i) => self.toroidal_hypotheses[*i].faces.clone(),
                SelectedSurface::BSpline(i) => self.bspline_hypotheses[*i].faces.clone(),
            })
            .collect()
    }
}
```
